\displaystyle 1+\Gamma(k)~\text{a.s.},~\forall\ k\geq 0.

(A.17)

It can be verified that

\displaystyle~~~\sup_{k\geq 0}\left(\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)\|^{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}\right|{\cal F}(k-1)\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}}

(A.18)

\displaystyle=\sup_{k\geq 0}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)\|^{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}}

(A.19)

\displaystyle=\sup_{k\geq 0}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(0)\mathcal{H}(0)\|^{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}}\leq\rho^{2}_{0}~\text{a.s.}

(A.20)

It follows from
\|\mathcal{H}(0)\|\leq\rho_{0}~\text{a.s.}
and Proposition
B.1
.(a) that
H^{*}_{j}(0)H_{j}(0)x\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
,
x\in\mathscr{X}
. For the integer
h>0
and
j\in\mathcal{V}
, we define the operator
\mathscr{H}_{j}:\mathscr{X}\to\mathscr{X}
by

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}_{j}(x)=h\mathbb{E}\left[H^{*}_{j}(0)H_{j}(0)x\right],~x\in\mathscr{X},~j\in\mathcal{V}.

(A.21)

For any given
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathscr{X}
and
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}_{j}(c_{1}x_{1}+c_{2}x_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{1}h\mathbb{E}\left[H^{*}_{j}(0)H_{j}(0)x_{1}\right]+c_{2}h\mathbb{E}\left[H^{*}_{j}(0)H_{j}(0)x_{2}\right]

(A.22)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{1}\mathscr{H}_{j}(x_{1})+c_{2}\mathscr{H}_{j}(x_{2}).

(A.23)

Noting that
H^{*}_{j}(0)H_{j}(0)x\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
, by Proposition 2.6.31 in
[
60
]
, we get

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathscr{H}_{j}(x_{1}),x_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle H^{*}_{j}(0)H_{j}(0)x_{1},x_{2}\right\rangle\right]

(A.24)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle x_{1},H^{*}_{j}(0)H_{j}(0)x_{2}\right\rangle\right]

(A.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle x_{1},\mathscr{H}_{j}(x_{2})\right\rangle,~j\in\mathcal{V}.

This together with (
A.22
) shows that
\mathscr{H}_{j}
is a linear self-adjoint operator, which together with (
A.21
) and

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{H}_{j}(x)\|\leq h\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|H^{*}_{j}(0)H_{j}(0)\right\|\|x\|\right]\leq h\rho_{0}^{2}\|x\|,~\forall\ x\in\mathscr{X},~j\in\mathcal{V}

gives that the self-adjoint operator
\mathscr{H}_{j}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X})
. Denote
\mathscr{H}_{j}(x):=\mathscr{H}_{j}x
,
\forall\ x\in\mathscr{X}
. Noting that
H^{*}_{j}(0)H_{j}(0)x\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
, it follows from Proposition 2.6.31 in
[
60
]
that

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathscr{H}_{j}x,x\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[H^{*}_{j}(0)H_{j}(0)x\right],x\right\rangle

(A.26)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle H^{*}_{j}(0)H_{j}(0)x,x\right\rangle\right]

(A.27)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|H_{j}(0)x\right\|^{2}\right]\geq 0,

(A.28)

from which we know that the operator
\mathscr{H}_{j}
defined in (
A.21
) is positive bounded linear self-adjoint,
j\in\mathcal{V}
.
For any given integers
k\geq 0
,
h>0
and
x\in\mathscr{X}
, if
A\in{\cal F}(kh-1)
, then it follows from Proposition
B.1
and (
A.18
) that
H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)(\boldsymbol{1}_{A}\otimes x)\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
,
i\geq kh
, which together with Lemma
B.3
implies that
\mathbb{E}[H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)(\boldsymbol{1}_{A}\otimes x)|{\cal F}(kh-1)]
uniquely exists. Since
\{\mathcal{H}(k),k\geq 0\}
and
\{v(k),k\geq 0\}
are both i.i.d sequences and they are mutually independent, from Definition
B.7
, it can be verified that
H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)x,\ j\geq kh
are independent of
\boldsymbol{1}_{F\cap A}
with
F\in{\cal F}(kh-1)
. Thus,

\displaystyle~~~\int_{F}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)(\boldsymbol{1}_{A}\otimes x)\right|{\cal F}(kh-1)\right]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle=\int_{F}\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)(\boldsymbol{1}_{A}\otimes x)\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)x\right)\boldsymbol{1}_{F\cap A}\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)x\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}\int_{\Omega}\boldsymbol{1}_{F\cap A}\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)x\right]\mathbb{P}(F\cap A)

\displaystyle=h\mathbb{E}\left[H^{*}_{j}(0)H_{j}(0)x\right]\mathbb{P}(F\cap A)

\displaystyle=h\int_{F}\mathbb{E}\left[H^{*}_{j}(0)H_{j}(0)x\right]\boldsymbol{1}_{A}\,\text{d}\mathbb{P},~\forall\ F\in{\cal F}(kh-1),~j\in\mathcal{V},

which gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)(\boldsymbol{1}_{A}\otimes x)\right|{\cal F}(kh-1)\right]=\mathscr{H}_{j}(\boldsymbol{1}_{A}\otimes x)~\text{a.s.},~j\in\mathcal{V}.

This together with the properties of the conditional expectation, the operator
\mathscr{H}_{j}
and the linearity of Bochner integral leads to

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}_{j}y=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)y\Bigg|{\cal F}(kh-1)\Bigg]~\text{a.s.},~j\in\mathcal{V},

(A.29)

where
y\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(kh-1);\mathscr{X})
is a simple function. For
f\in L^{2}(\Omega,{\cal F}(kh-1);\mathscr{X})
, by Lemma
B.1
, we know that there exists a sequence of simple functions
\{f_{n}\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(kh-1);\mathscr{X}),n\geq 0\}
satisfying
\|f_{n}\|\leq\|f\|\text{a.s.}
and
\lim_{n\to\infty}f_{n}=f~\text{a.s.}
, which together with (
A.18
) and Cauchy inequality gives
H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)f\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
. Thus, from Lemma
B.3
, it is known that
\mathbb{E}[H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)f|{\cal F}(kh-1)]
uniquely exists. Noting that
\mathscr{H}_{j}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X})
, it follows from (
A.18
)-(
A.29
) and the dominated convergence theorem of conditional expectation that

\displaystyle~~~\mathscr{H}_{j}f

(A.30)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{H}_{j}f_{n}

(A.31)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)f_{n}\right|{\cal F}(kh-1)\right]

(A.32)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)\lim_{n\to\infty}f_{n}\right|{\cal F}(kh-1)\right]

(A.33)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)f\right|{\cal F}(kh-1)\right]~\text{a.s.},~j\in\mathcal{V}.

(A.34)

Let
\{x(k),{\cal F}(kh-1),k\geq 0\}
be a
L_{2}
-bounded adaptive sequence with values in the Hilbert space
\mathscr{X}
. Then, by (
A.30
), we get

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}_{j}x(k)=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}H^{*}_{j}(i)H_{j}(i)x(k)\right|{\cal F}(kh-1)\right]~\text{a.s.},~j\in\mathcal{V}.

For any non-zero element
x
in Hilbert space
x
, from Proposition 2.6.31 in
[
60
]
, the condition (
25
) and (
A.21
), we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathscr{H}_{j}x,x\right\rangle=h\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{N}H^{*}_{j}(0)H_{j}(0)x\right],x\right\rangle=h\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{j}(0)x\|^{2}\right]>0.

This together with (
A
)-(
A.18
) and Theorem
3.2
gives that the algorithm (
11
) is both mean square and almost surely strongly consistent.

\hfill\qed

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

We will briefly sketch the proof. Firstly, by the measurability and the integration theory of mappings with values in Banach spaces, we prove that, for any
i\geq kh
,
\mathbb{E}[K_{x_{j}(i)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(i)}|{\cal F}(kh-1)]
exists. Then, we develop a property of the conditional expectations of operator-valued random elements. By the property and the reproducing property of RKHS, we verify the conditions in Theorem
3.2
, thereby deriving the conclusion of the theorem.

It follows from Assumption
4.2
and Lemma
4.1
that Assumption
3.1
holds. By Assumption
4.3
, it is known that
\{v(k),{\cal F}(k),k\geq 0\}
is the martingale difference sequence and there exists a constant
\beta_{v}:=N\beta>0
, such that
\sup\limits_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\big[\|v(k)\|^{2}|{\cal F}(k-1)\big]\leq N\max_{i\in\mathcal{V}}\sup\limits_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\big[\|v_{i}(k)\|^{2}|{\cal F}(k-1)\big]\leq\beta_{v}~\text{a.s.},

which implies that Assumption
3.2
holds.
Let
x\in\mathscr{X}
. Then, for any given
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathscr{H}_{K}
and
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, we get

\displaystyle\left(K_{x}\otimes K_{x}\right)(c_{1}f_{1}+c_{2}f_{2})

(A.35)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{1}f_{1}(x)K_{x}+c_{2}f_{2}(x)K_{x}

(A.36)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{1}\left(K_{x}\otimes K_{x}\right)f_{1}+c_{2}\left(K_{x}\otimes K_{x}\right)f_{2}.

(A.37)

From Assumption
4.1
and the reproducing property of
\mathscr{H}_{K}
, we know that
\left\|\left(K_{x}\otimes K_{x}\right)f\right\|_{K}\leq\|K_{x}\|_{K}^{2}\|f\|_{K}=K(x,x)\|f\|_{K}\leq\sup_{x\in\mathscr{X}}K(x,x)\|f\|_{K},~\forall\ f\in\mathscr{H}_{K},~\forall\ x\in\mathscr{X}.

Thus, it follows from (
A.35
) that
K_{x}\otimes K_{x}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K})
. Let
x=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes x_{i}
be a random vector with values in the Hilbert space
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
, where
A_{i}\cap A_{j}=\emptyset
,
1\leq i\neq j\leq n
. Noting that
K_{x}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes K_{x_{i}}
, we have
K_{x}\otimes K_{x}=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes K_{x_{i}}\right)\otimes\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes K_{x_{i}}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes\left(K_{x_{i}}\otimes K_{x_{i}}\right),

thus,
K_{x}\otimes K_{x}
is a simple function with values in the Banach space
\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K})
. For any given random vector
x
with values in the Hilbert space
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
, it is known that there exists a simple function sequence
\{x_{n},n\geq 0\}
with values in
\mathscr{X}
, such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|x-x_{n}\|=0~\text{a.s.}

This together with the reproducing property of
\mathscr{H}_{K}
and the continuity of kernel
K
gives

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|K_{x}-K_{x_{n}}\right\|_{K}=0~\text{a.s.}

(A.38)

It follows from the reproducing property of
\mathscr{H}_{K}
that

\displaystyle\left\|K_{x}\otimes K_{x}-K_{x_{n}}\otimes K_{x_{n}}\right\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K})}

(A.39)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|(K_{x}-K_{x_{n}})\otimes K_{x}\right\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K})}+\left\|K_{x_{n}}\otimes(K_{x}-K_{x_{n}})\right\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K})}

(A.40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\|f\|_{K}=1}\left\|\left((K_{x}-K_{x_{n}})\otimes K_{x}\right)f\right\|_{K}+\sup_{\|f\|_{K}=1}\left\|\left(K_{x_{n}}\otimes(K_{x}-K_{x_{n}})\right)f\right\|_{K}

(A.41)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\|f\|_{K}=1}\left\|f(x)(K_{x}-K_{x_{n}})\right\|_{K}+\sup_{\|f\|_{K}=1}\left\|(f(x)-f(x_{n}))K_{x_{n}}\right\|_{K}

(A.42)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|K_{x}\|_{K}\|K_{x}-K_{x_{n}}\|_{K}+\|K_{x_{n}}\|_{K}\|K_{x}-K_{x_{n}}\|_{K}\leq 2\sup_{x\in\mathscr{X}}\sqrt{K(x,x)}\|K_{x}-K_{x_{n}}\|_{K}~\text{a.s.}

Then, by Assumption
4.1
and (
A.38
), we have
\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|K_{x}\otimes K_{x}-K_{x_{n}}\otimes K_{x_{n}}\right\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K})}=0~\text{a.s.}

Noting that
K_{x_{n}}\otimes K_{x_{n}}
is a simple function with values in Banach space
\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K})
, by Lemma
B.1
, it is known that
K_{x}\otimes K_{x}
is strongly
\mathbb{P}
-measurable with respect to the topology
\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K}))
, and therefore
K_{x}\otimes K_{x}
is a random element with values in Banach space
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K}),\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K})))
. Thus, by Assumption
4.1
, we get
K_{x_{j}(i)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(i)}\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K}))
, which together with Lemma
B.3
gives that
\mathbb{E}[K_{x_{j}(i)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(i)}|{\cal F}(kh-1)]
uniquely exists. Let
\{g(k),{\cal F}(kh-1),k\geq 0\}
be the
L_{2}
-bounded adaptive sequence with values in
\mathscr{H}_{K}
, by Assumption
4.1
, Proposition
B.4
and the condition (
33
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg\|\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[H_{j}^{*}(i)H_{j}(i)g(k)\right|{\cal F}(kh-1)\right]-N_{j}g(k)\bigg\|_{K}^{2}\Bigg]

(A.43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg\|\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[K_{x_{j}(i)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(i)}g(k)\right|{\cal F}(kh-1)\right]-N_{j}g(k)\bigg\|_{K}^{2}\Bigg]

(A.44)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg\|\Big(\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[K_{x_{j}(i)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(i)}\right|{\cal F}(kh-1)\right]-N_{j}\Big)g(k)\bigg\|_{K}^{2}\Bigg]<\infty.

(A.45)

Denote
\rho_{0}=N\left(\sup_{x\in\mathscr{X}}K(x,x)\right)^{2^{\max\{h,2\}-1}}
.
Given the integer
h>0
, by Assumption
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)\|_{\mathscr{L}\left(\mathscr{H}_{K}^{N}\right)}^{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}\right|{\cal F}(k-1)\right]

(A.46)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\sup_{i\in\mathcal{V}}\left\|K_{x_{i}(k)}\otimes K_{x_{i}(k)}\right\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K})}^{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}\right|{\cal F}(k-1)\right]

(A.47)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\sup_{i\in\mathcal{V}}\sup_{\|f\|_{K}=1}\left\|f(x_{i}(k))K_{x_{i}(k)}\right\|_{K}^{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}\right|{\cal F}(k-1)\right]

(A.48)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sup_{i\in\mathcal{V}}\sup_{\|f\|_{K}=1}|f(x_{i}(k))|^{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}\left\|K_{x_{i}(k)}\right\|_{K}^{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}\Bigg|{\cal F}(k-1)\Bigg]

(A.49)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sup_{i\in\mathcal{V}}\sup_{\|f\|_{K}=1}\left\|f\right\|^{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}_{K}\left(\sup_{x\in\mathscr{X}}K(x,x)\right)^{2^{\max\{h,2\}-1}}\Bigg|{\cal F}(k-1)\Bigg]

(A.50)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\rho_{0}~\text{a.s.},

which gives

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0}\left(\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)\|_{\mathscr{L}\left(\mathscr{H}_{K}^{N}\right)}^{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}\right|{\cal F}(k-1)\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}}\leq\rho_{0}~\text{a.s.}

(A.51)

It follows from Condition
3.2
that there exists a constant
j_{0}>0
and an integer
t_{0}>0
, such that
\sup_{k\geq 0}(4\rho_{0}a(k)+4\|\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\|b(k))\leq j_{0}
and
\sup_{k\geq t_{0}}(a(k)+b(k))(4\rho_{0}+4\|\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\|)\leq 1
. Noting that
\|4a(k)\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)+4b(k)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{H}_{K}}\|_{\mathscr{L}\left(\mathscr{H}_{K}^{N}\right)}\leq j_{0}
if
k<t_{0}
, and
\|4a(k)\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)+4b(k)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{H}_{K}}\|_{\mathscr{L}\left(\mathscr{H}_{K}^{N}\right)}\leq 1
, otherwise.
Then, we get

\displaystyle\big\|I_{\mathscr{H}_{K}^{N}}-4\left(a(k)\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)+b(k)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{H}_{K}}\right)\big\|_{\mathscr{L}\left(\mathscr{H}_{K}^{N}\right)}\leq 1+\Gamma(k)~\text{a.s.},

(A.52)

where

\displaystyle\Gamma(k)=\begin{cases}j_{0},&k<t_{0},\\
0,&k\geq t_{0},\end{cases}

and satisfies
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Gamma(k)<\infty
. It follows from (
A.52
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|I_{\mathscr{H}_{K}^{N}}-4(a(k)\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)+b(k)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{H}_{K}})\Big\|_{\mathscr{L}\left(\mathscr{H}_{K}^{N}\right)}\big|{\cal F}(kh-1)\Big]\leq 1+\Gamma(k)~\text{a.s.}

Hence, combining (
A.43
)-(
A.51
), the above inequality and Theorem
3.2
gives that the algorithm (
30
) is mean square and almost surely strongly consistent.

\hfill\qed

Proof of Corollary
4.1
.

It follows from Assumption
4.1
and Theorem
4.1
that
K_{x_{j}(i)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(i)}\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K}))
, which together with Lemma
B.3
implies that
\mathbb{E}[K_{x_{j}(i)}\otimes

K_{x_{j}(i)}|{\cal F}(kh-1)]
uniquely exists. Let
\{g(k),{\cal F}(kh-1),k\geq 0\}
be a
L_{2}
-bounded adaptive sequence with values in
\mathscr{H}_{K}
, by the condition (
34
), we get

\displaystyle~~~~\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\Bigg\|\Bigg(N_{j}-\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[K_{x_{j}(i)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(i)}\right|{\cal F}(kh-1)\right]\Bigg)g(k)\Bigg\|_{K}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\Bigg\|N_{j}-\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[K_{x_{j}(i)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(i)}\right|{\cal F}(kh-1)\right]\Bigg\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K})}^{2}\|g(k)\|_{K}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{j\in\mathcal{V}}\Bigg\|N_{j}-\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[K_{x_{j}(i)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(i)}\right|{\cal F}(kh-1)\right]\Bigg\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K})}^{2}\|g(k)\|_{K}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq N\mu_{0}\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|g(k)\|_{K}^{2}\right]\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\tau(k)<\infty,

where the last inequality is obtained from
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\tau(k)<\infty
. This together with Theorem
4.1
implies that the algorithm (
30
) is both mean square and almost surely strongly consistent.

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the reproducing property of
\mathscr{H}_{K}
, we have

\displaystyle|f_{i}(k)(x)-f_{0}(x)|=|\langle f_{i}(k)-f_{0},K_{x}\rangle|\leq\left\|f_{i}(k)-f_{0}\right\|_{K}\left\|K_{x}\right\|_{K},\ \forall\ x\in\mathscr{X},\ i\in\mathcal{V}.

Noting that algorithm (
30
) is almost surely strongly consistent and by the above inequality, we have
\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}f_{i}(k)(x)=f_{0}(x)

a.s.
,
\forall\ x\in\mathscr{X},\ i\in\mathcal{V}
.

\hfill\qed

Proof of Corollary
4.2
.

Noting that
\{(x_{1}(k),\cdots,x_{N}(k)),k\geq 0\}
and
\{(v_{1}(k),\cdots,v_{N}(k)),k\geq 0\}
are both i.i.d. sequences and they are mutually independent, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[K_{x_{j}(i)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(i)}\right|{\cal F}(kh-1)\right]=\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left[K_{x_{j}(i)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(i)}\right]=h\mathbb{E}\left[K_{x_{j}(0)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(0)}\right].

Denote
N_{j}:\mathscr{H}_{K}\to\mathscr{H}_{K}
by

N_{j}=h\mathbb{E}\left[K_{x_{j}(0)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(0)}\right],~j\in\mathcal{V}.

It follows from Proposition 2.6.31 in
[
60
]
that
N_{j}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K})
is a positive self-adjoint operator. Noting that

\displaystyle\bigg\|N_{j}-\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[K_{x_{j}(i)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(i)}\right|{\cal F}(kh-1)\right]\bigg\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K})}^{2}=0~\text{a.s.},

by the condition (
35
), we get

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{N}N_{j}f,f\right\rangle_{K}=h\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{N}K_{x_{j}(0)}\otimes K_{x_{j}(0)}\right]f,f\right\rangle_{K}>0,

where
f
is an arbitrary non-zero function in
\mathscr{H}_{K}
. Hence, it follows from Corollary
4.1
that the algorithm (
30
) is both mean square and almost surely strongly consistent.

\hfill\qed

Appendix B
Theoretical Framework of Random Elements with Values in a Topological Space

The proposed algorithm involves the sequences of random forward operators induced by random input data. Conventionally, a random element with values in a Banach space is required to be strongly measurable, which is almost separably valued with respect to the topology induced by the norm in the Banach space (
[
61
]
). It is known that operator-valued mappings may not be strongly measurable since the Banach space of bounded linear operators is generally non-separable with respect to the uniform operator topology (the topology induced by the operator norm) (
[
60
]
). In this section, we develop a self-contained theoretical framework of random elements with values in a topological space.

B-A

Random Elements with Values in a Topological Space

Definition B.1
.

Let
(\Omega,{\cal F},\mathbb{P})
be a complete probability space, and
(\mathscr{U},\tau)
be a topological space. Given the mapping
f:\Omega\to\mathscr{U}
, if there exists a separable closed subset
\mathscr{U}_{0}
of
\mathscr{U}
and a subset
\Omega_{0}
of
\Omega
with probability measure
1
, such that

f(\Omega_{0}):=\{f(x):x\in\Omega_{0}\}\subseteq\mathscr{U}_{0},

then
f
is called almost separably valued with respect to
\tau
.

Definition B.2
.

Let
(\Omega,{\cal F},\mathbb{P})
be a complete probability space and
(\mathscr{U},\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{U};\tau))
be a measurable space, where
\tau
is the topology on
\mathscr{U}
, and
\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{U};\tau)
is the Borel
\sigma
-algebra of the topological space
(\mathscr{U},\tau)
, i.e., the smallest
\sigma
-algebra containing all open sets in
\mathscr{U}
. A mapping
f:\Omega\to\mathscr{U}
is said to be strongly
\mathbb{P}
-measurable or to be a random element with values in the topological space
(\mathscr{U},\tau)
if it is
{\cal F}/\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{U};\tau)
-measurable and almost separably valued with respect to
\tau
.

Definition B.3
.

If
f
is a random element with values in the topological space
(\mathscr{U},\tau)
, then the distribution of
f
is defined by the Borel probability measure
\mu_{f}(B):=\mathbb{P}(f^{-1}(B))
on
(\mathscr{U},\tau)
,
\forall\ B\in\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{U};\tau)
.

The following lemma is derived directly from Proposition 1.1.16 in
[
60
]
.

Lemma B.1
.

Given a mapping
f:\Omega\to\mathscr{X}
with values in Banach space
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
,
f
is strongly
\mathbb{P}
-measurable if and only if
there exists a sequence of simple functions
\{f_{n}:\Omega\to\mathscr{X},n\geq 1\}
such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|f_{n}-f\|_{\mathscr{X}}=0
with probability
1
.

It follows from Definition
B.2
that for a separable Banach space
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
,
any
{\cal F}/\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{X};\\
\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
-measurable mapping
f:\Omega\to\mathscr{X}
is a random element with values in
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
.

Combining Definition
B.2
and Corollary 1.4.7, Proposition 1.1.28 and Corollary 1.1.29 in
[
60
]
, we directly obtain the following properties of operator-valued random elements.

Proposition B.1
.

Let
f_{0}:\Omega\to\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})
,
f_{1}:\Omega\to\mathscr{X}
,
f_{2}:\Omega\to\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})
,
g_{1}:\Omega\to\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})
and
g_{2}:\Omega\to\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})
.

(a). If
f_{0}
is a random element with values in
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}),\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})))
, then the mapping
f_{0}y:\omega\mapsto f_{0}(\omega)y
is a random element with values in
(\mathscr{Z},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Z}))
for arbitrary
y\in\mathscr{Y}
.

(b). If
f_{1}
is strongly
\mathbb{P}
-measurable with respect to
\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X})
and
g_{1}x:\Omega\to\mathscr{Y}
is strongly
\mathbb{P}
-measurable with respect to
\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y})
,
\forall\ x\in\mathscr{X}
, then
g_{1}f_{1}:\Omega\to\mathscr{Y}
is strongly
\mathbb{P}
-measurable with respect to
\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y})
.

(c). For arbitrary
x\in\mathscr{X}
and
y\in\mathscr{Y}
, if the mapping
f_{2}x:\Omega\to\mathscr{Y}
is strongly
\mathbb{P}
-measurable with respect to
\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y})
and the mapping
g_{2}y:\Omega\to\mathscr{Z}
is strongly
\mathbb{P}
-measurable with respect to
\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Z})
, then the mapping
(g_{2}f_{2})x^{\prime}:\Omega\to\mathscr{Z}
is strongly
\mathbb{P}
-measurable with respect to
\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Z})
for arbitrary
x^{\prime}\in\mathscr{X}
.

If the operator-valued mapping
f:\Omega\to\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})
is strongly
\mathbb{P}
-measurable with respect to the uniform operator topology
\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}))
, then for arbitrary
y\in\mathscr{Y}
,
fy:\Omega\to\mathscr{Z}
is strongly
\mathbb{P}
-measurable with respect to
\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Z})
. Noting that
\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}))\subseteq\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}))
,
it follows that a random element with values in
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}),\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})))
is a random element with values in

(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}),\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})))
. For the measurability of mappings with values in the space of operators with different topologies, one may refer to
[
60
]
and
[
62
]
-
[
63
]
.

For a random element
f:\Omega\to\mathscr{X}
with values in
(\mathscr{X},\tau(\mathscr{X}))
, we denote the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
f
in the sense of the topology
\tau(\mathscr{X})
by

\sigma(f;\tau(\mathscr{X})):=\left\{f^{-1}(B):B\in\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{X};\tau(\mathscr{X}))\right\}.

Based on the above definitions, we have the following proposition.

Proposition B.2
.

If
f:\Omega\rightarrow{\mathscr{X}}
is a random element with values in a separable Hilbert space
(\mathscr{X},\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathscr{X}})
,
T:\Omega\rightarrow{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})}
is a random element with values in a topological
space
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y}),\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y}))),
and
(\mathscr{Y},\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}})
is a separable Hilbert space, then
Tf:\omega\rightarrow{T(\omega)f(\omega)}
satisfies

\displaystyle\sigma(Tf;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y}))\subseteq\sigma(T;\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y}))\bigvee\sigma(f;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X})).

(B.1)

The proof of the above proposition needs the following lemma.

Lemma B.2
.

If
y:\Omega\rightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
is a random element with values in a separable Hilbert space
(\mathscr{Y},\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}})
with an orthonormal basis
\{f_{j},\ j\geq 1\}
, then

\displaystyle\sigma(y;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y}))=\bigvee\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sigma\left(\langle y,f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)\right).

(B.2)

Proof.

At first, we prove

\displaystyle\sigma(y;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y}))\subseteq\bigvee\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sigma\left(\langle y,f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)\right).

(B.3)

For any
r>0,\ s\in\mathscr{Y},
we have

\displaystyle\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\|y(\omega)-s\|_{\mathscr{Y}}<r\right\}=

\displaystyle\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\|y(\omega)-s\|_{\mathscr{Y}}^{2}<r^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\langle y(\omega)-s,f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}}^{2}<r^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left(\langle y(\omega),f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}}-\langle s,f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}}\right)^{2}<r^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle\bigvee\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sigma\left(\left(\langle y(\omega),f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}}-\langle s,f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}}\right)^{2};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\bigvee\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sigma\left(\langle y(\omega),f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)\right).

Therefore, we know that for any
r>0,\ s\in\mathscr{Y},

\{\omega\in\Omega:\|y(\omega)-s\|_{\mathscr{Y}}<r\}\in\bigvee\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sigma(\langle y,f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\\
\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right))
. This together with the definitions of
\sigma(y;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y}))
and
\bigvee\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sigma\left(\langle y,f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)\right)

gives (
B.3
).
Next, we prove

\displaystyle\bigvee\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sigma\left(\langle y,f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)\right)\subseteq\sigma(y;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y})).

(B.4)

For
j=1,2,\ldots
, denote
h_{j}(y)=\langle y,f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}}
, which is
a continuous linear functional and is measurable with respect to
y
.
Then, for any
A\in\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)),
we have

\displaystyle\{\omega\in\Omega:\langle y(\omega),f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}}\in\mathit{A}\}=\{\omega\in\Omega:y(\omega)\in h_{j}^{-1}(\mathit{A})\}\in\sigma(y;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y})),\ j=1,2,\ldots

Then, we have
\sigma\left(\langle y,f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)\right)\subseteq\sigma(y;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y})),\ j=1,2,\ldots
This together with the definition of
\sigma
-algebra gives (
B.4
). Combining (
B.3
) and (
B.4
), we have (
B.2
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
B.2
:

Suppose that
\{e_{i},\ i\geq 1\}
and
\{f_{j},\ j\geq 1\}
are the orthonormal bases of
\mathscr{X}
and
\mathscr{Y}
, respectively.
By Lemma
B.2
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma(Tf;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y}))=\bigvee\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sigma\left(\langle Tf,f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)\right).

(B.5)

By the linearity of the inner product, we have

\displaystyle\langle Tf,f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle T\left(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\infty}\langle f,e_{i}\rangle_{\mathscr{X}}e_{i}\right),f_{j}\right\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\infty}\langle f,e_{i}\rangle_{\mathscr{X}}Te_{i},f_{j}\right\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\infty}\langle f,e_{i}\rangle_{\mathscr{X}}\left\langle Te_{i},f_{j}\right\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}},\ j=1,2,\ldots

Note that for any
i=1,2,\ldots
and
j=1,2,\ldots
,
\sigma\left(\langle f,e_{i}\rangle_{\mathscr{X}}\left\langle Te_{i},f_{j}\right\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)\right)\subseteq\sigma(\langle f,e_{i}\rangle_{\mathscr{X}};\\
\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathbb{R}))\bigvee\sigma(\left\langle Te_{i},f_{j}\right\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathbb{R}))\subseteq\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma(\langle f,e_{i}\rangle_{\mathscr{X}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right))\right)\bigvee\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma(\left\langle Te_{i},f_{j}\right\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right))\right)
. This together with the above equality gives

\displaystyle\sigma(\langle Tf,f_{j}\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma\left(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\infty}\langle f,e_{i}\rangle_{\mathscr{X}}\left\langle Te_{i},f_{j}\right\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma\left(\langle f,e_{i}\rangle_{\mathscr{X}}\left\langle Te_{i},f_{j}\right\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma(\langle f,e_{i}\rangle_{\mathscr{X}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right))\right)\bigvee\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma(\left\langle Te_{i},f_{j}\right\rangle_{\mathscr{Y}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right))\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma(\langle f,e_{i}\rangle_{\mathscr{X}};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right))\right)\bigvee\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma(Te_{i};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathscr{Y}\right))\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\sigma(f;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))\bigvee\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma(Te_{i};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathscr{Y}\right))\right),\ j=1,2,\ldots,

which together with (
B.5
) leads to

\displaystyle\sigma(Tf;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y}))\subseteq\sigma(f;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))\bigvee\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma(Te_{i};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathscr{Y}\right))\right).

(B.6)

Now, we prove
\sigma(Te_{i};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathscr{Y}\right))\subseteq\sigma(T;\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})),\ i=1,2,\ldots

For any
i=1,2,\ldots
and
y\in\mathscr{Y}
, define
T_{i,y}:\mathscr{X}\to\mathscr{Y}
as
T_{i,y}h=\langle h,e_{i}\rangle_{\mathscr{X}}y,\ h\in\mathscr{X}
. Notice that
T_{i,y}e_{i}=y
and
T_{i,y}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})
. Then, for any
r>0
, we have

\displaystyle\{\omega\in\Omega:\|T(\omega)e_{i}-y\|_{\mathscr{Y}}<r\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\omega\in\Omega:\|T(\omega)e_{i}-T_{i,y}e_{i}\|_{\mathscr{Y}}<r\}.

Then, we know that

\displaystyle\left(Te_{i}\right)^{-1}(\{x\in\mathscr{Y}:\|x-y\|_{\mathscr{Y}}<r\})=T^{-1}(\{H\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y}):\|He_{i}-T_{i,y}e_{i}\|_{\mathscr{Y}}<r\}).

(B.7)

Denote
O=\{H\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y}):\|He_{i}-T_{i,y}e_{i}\|_{\mathscr{Y}}<r\}
.
For any
H\in O
and
S_{H}\in\big\{K\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y}):\|Ke_{i}-He_{i}\|_{\mathscr{Y}}<\frac{r-\|He_{i}-T_{i,y}e_{i}\|_{\mathscr{Y}}}{2}\big\},
we have

\|S_{H}e_{i}-T_{i,y}e_{i}\|_{\mathscr{Y}}\leq\|S_{H}e_{i}-He_{i}\|_{\mathscr{Y}}+\|He_{i}-T_{i,y}e_{i}\|_{\mathscr{Y}}<\frac{r+\|He_{i}-T_{i,y}e_{i}\|_{\mathscr{Y}}}{2}<r

and then
S_{H}\in O
. Therefore, for any
H\in O
, there exists
\epsilon=\frac{r-\|He_{i}-T_{i,y}e_{i}\|_{\mathscr{Y}}}{2}
and
e_{i}\in\mathscr{X}
, such that

\left\{K\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y}):\|Ke_{i}-He_{i}\|_{\mathscr{Y}}<\epsilon\right\}\subseteq O.

This together with the definition of an open set in
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y}),\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})))
gives that
\{H\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y}):\|He_{i}-T_{i,y}e_{i}\|_{\mathscr{Y}}<r\}
is an open set in
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y}),\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})))
. Note that
\{x\in\mathscr{Y}:\|x-y\|_{\mathscr{Y}}<r\}
is also an open set in
(\mathscr{Y},\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathscr{Y}\right))
. Then, by (
B.7
) and the definitions of
\sigma(Te_{i};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathscr{Y}\right))
and
\sigma(T;\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y}))
, we have
\sigma(Te_{i};\tau_{\text{N}}\left(\mathscr{Y}\right))\subseteq\sigma(T;\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})),i=1,2,\ldots
This together with (
B.6
) gives (
B.1
).

\hfill\qed

B-B

Conditional Expectation

Definition B.4

(
[
61
]
)
.

If
f\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
, then the mathematical expectation of
f
is defined as the Bochner integral

\mathbb{E}[f]=(\text{B})\int_{\Omega}f\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}.

Without raising ambiguity, the Bochner integral in this paper will omit the capital letter (B) in front of the integral symbol.

Definition B.5

(
[
60
]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{G}\subseteq{\cal F}
be a sub-
\sigma
-algebra,
f\in L^{0}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
and
g\in L^{0}(\Omega,\mathscr{G};\mathscr{X})
. If

\displaystyle\int_{F}g\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{F}f\,\text{d}\mathbb{P},~\forall\ F\in\mathscr{G}_{f}\cap\mathscr{G}_{g},

where
\mathscr{G}_{\phi}:=\{F\in\mathscr{G}:\boldsymbol{1}_{F}\phi\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})\}
,
\phi\in L^{0}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
, then
g
is called the conditional expectation of
f
with respect to
\mathscr{G}
.

Definition B.6

(
[
60
]
)
.

Let
\{{\cal F}_{k},k\geq 0\}
be a family of sub-
\sigma
-algebras of
{\cal F}
and
\{f_{k},k\geq 0\}
be a family of random elements with values in the Hilbert space
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
.

1.

If
{\cal F}_{n}\subseteq{\cal F}_{m}
,
\forall\ m\geq n\geq 0
, then
\{{\cal F}_{k},k\geq 0\}
is called a filter in
(\Omega,{\cal F},\mathbb{P})
.

2.

If
\{{\cal F}_{k},k\geq 0\}
is a filter in
(\Omega,{\cal F},\mathbb{P})
,
f_{k}\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}_{k};\mathscr{X})
,
\forall\ k\geq 0
, then
\{f_{k},{\cal F}_{k},k\geq 0\}
is called an adaptive sequence.

3.

If
\{f_{k},{\cal F}_{k},k\geq 0\}
is an adaptive sequence,
f_{k}
is Bochner integrable on
{\cal F}_{k-1}
and

\mathbb{E}[f_{k}|{\cal F}_{k-1}]=0,\ \forall\ k\geq 0,

then
\{f_{k},{\cal F}_{k},k\geq 0\}
is called a sequence of martingale differences.

It can be verified that if
\{f_{k},{\cal F}_{k},k\geq 0\}
is an adaptive sequence with values in a separable Hilbert space
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
, then
\{\|f_{k}\|_{\mathscr{X}},{\cal F}_{k},k\geq 0\}
is an adaptive sequence with values in
(\mathbb{R},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathbb{R}))
.

Lemma B.3

(
[
60
]
)
.

If
\mathscr{G}\subseteq{\cal F}
is a sub-
\sigma
-algebra and
f\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
, then there exists a unique conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]\in L^{0}(\Omega,\mathscr{G};\mathscr{X})\cap L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
satisfying

\int_{A}\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{A}f\,\text{d}\mathbb{P},~\forall\ A\in\mathscr{G}.

For a Banach space
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
,
by Lemma
B.3
, it follows that there exists a unique conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]\in L^{0}(\Omega,\mathscr{G};\mathscr{X})
of the Bochner integrable random element
f
with values in
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
, and
\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]
is a random element with values in
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
.
We have the following propositions of conditional expectations.

Proposition B.3
.

If
f\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}))
is a random element with values in Banach space
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}),\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}))
, then
fy\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{Z})
is a random element with values in Banach space
(\mathscr{Z},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Z}))
, and
\mathbb{E}[fy]=\mathbb{E}[f]y
,
\forall\ y\in\mathscr{Y}
.

Proof.

Since the random elements with values in the Banach space
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})
,
\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})))
are certainly the random elements with values in
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})
,
\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})))
, it follows that
f\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}))
implies
fy\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{Z})
,
\forall\ y\in\mathscr{Y}
by Proposition (
B.1
).(a). Considering the simple function
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes T_{i}
with values in Banach space
\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})
, where
A_{i}\in{\cal F}
,
T_{i}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})
,
A_{i}\cap A_{j}=\emptyset
,
1\leq i\neq j\leq n
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes T_{i}\right)y\right]=\mathbb{P}(A)(T_{i}y)=(\mathbb{P}(A_{i})T_{i})y=\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes T_{i}\right]y,~1\leq i\leq n,

which leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes T_{i}\right)y\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes T_{i})y\right]

(B.8)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\ 1_{A_{i}}\otimes T_{i}\right]y

(B.9)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes T_{i}\right]y.

(B.10)

Since
f\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}))
is a random element with values in the Banach space
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}),\\
\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})))
, it follows from Lemma
B.1
that there exists a sequence of simple functions
\{T_{n},n\geq 1\}
such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|f-T_{n}\|=0~\text{a.s.}
and
\|T_{n}\|\leq\|f\|~\text{a.s.}
Noting that
\|f-T_{n}\|\leq 2\|f\|\in L^{1}(\Omega)
and
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|fy-T_{n}y\|=0~\text{a.s.}
,
\forall\ y\in\mathscr{Y}
, by the dominated convergence theorem, it follows that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[(f-T_{n})y]=0
,
\forall\ y\in\mathscr{Y}
, and
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[T_{n}]=\mathbb{E}[f]
. Thus, it follows from (
B.8
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[fy]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[(f-T_{n})y]+\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[T_{n}y]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[T_{n}]y=\mathbb{E}[f]y,~\forall\ y\in\mathscr{Y}.

∎

Proposition B.4
.

If
f\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}))
is a random element with values in the Banach space
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}),\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})))
, and
y\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathscr{G};\mathscr{Y})
is a random element with values in the Banach space
(\mathscr{Y},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y}))
, where
\mathscr{G}
is a sub-
\sigma
-algebra of
{\cal F}
, then
fy\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{Z})
is a random element with values in the Banach space
(\mathscr{Z},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Z}))
and
\mathbb{E}[fy|\mathscr{G}]=\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]y~\text{a.s.}

Proof.

Since the random elements with values in the Banach space
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})
,
\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})))
are the random elements with values in the topological space
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})
,
\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z})))
, it follows from Proposition
B.1
.(b) that
fy
is a random element with values in the Banach space
(\mathscr{Z},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Z}))
,
f\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}))
, and
y\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathscr{Y})
implies
fy\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{Z})
. Consider the simple function
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes y_{i}
with values in the Banach space
\mathscr{Y}
, where
A_{i}\in\mathscr{G}
,
y_{i}\in\mathscr{Y}
,
A_{i}\cap A_{j}=\emptyset
,
1\leq i\neq j\leq n
. For any given
F\in\mathscr{G}
and
1\leq i\leq n
, by Lemma
B.3
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{F}\mathbb{E}[f(\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes y_{i})|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{F}f(\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes y_{i})\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{F\cap A_{i}}fy_{i}\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\mathbb{E}[(\boldsymbol{1}_{F\cap A_{i}}f)y_{i}].

(B.11)

Noting that
F\cap A_{i}\in\mathscr{G}
, by Lemma
B.3
and Proposition
B.3
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\boldsymbol{1}_{F\cap A_{i}}f)y_{i}]=\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{F\cap A_{i}}f]y_{i}=\left(\int_{F\cap A_{i}}f\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}\right)y_{i}=\left(\int_{F\cap A_{i}}\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}\right)y_{i}.

Noting that
\boldsymbol{1}_{F\cap A_{i}}\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathscr{G};\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}))
, it follows from Lemma
B.3
and Proposition
B.3
that

\displaystyle\left(\int_{F\cap A_{i}}\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}\right)y_{i}=\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{1}_{F\cap A_{i}}\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]y_{i}\right]=\int_{F}\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}](\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes y_{i})\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}.

(B.12)

Noting that
\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}](\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes y_{i})\in L^{0}(\Omega,\mathscr{G};\mathscr{Z})
, it follows from (
B.11
)-(
B.12
) and Lemma
B.3
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left.\left[f\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes y_{i}\right)\right|\mathscr{G}\right]=\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes y_{i}\right)~\text{a.s.}

(B.13)

Given a random element
y\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathscr{G};\mathscr{Y})
with values in the Banach space
(\mathscr{Y},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y}))
, there exists a sequence of simple functions
\{y_{n}\in L^{0}(\Omega,\mathscr{G};\mathscr{Y}),n\geq 1\}
with values in the Banach space
\mathscr{Y}
such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|y-y_{n}\|=0~\text{a.s.}
and
\|y_{n}\|\leq\|y\|~\text{a.s.}
Noting that
\|fy_{n}\|\leq\|f\|^{2}+\|y\|^{2}\in L^{1}(\Omega)
and
\mathbb{E}[f|\mathcal{G}]\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathscr{G};\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\mathscr{Z}))
, by the dominated convergence theorem of conditional expectation and (
B.13
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[fy|\mathscr{G}]=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[f\left(\lim_{n\to\infty}y_{n}\right)\right|\mathscr{G}\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[fy_{n}\right|\mathscr{G}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[f\right|\mathscr{G}\right]\left(\lim_{n\to\infty}y_{n}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[f\right|\mathscr{G}\right]y~\text{a.s.}

∎

B-C

Independence and Conditional Independence

In terms of the independence and conditional independence of random elements with values in a topological space, we have the following definitions.

Definition B.7
.

Let
\mathscr{I}
be a set of indices,
f_{j}
,
j\in\mathscr{I}
be the random elements with values in
(\mathscr{X}_{j},\tau(\mathscr{X}_{j}))
. If

\mathbb{P}\left(f_{\alpha_{1}}\in B_{1},\cdots,f_{\alpha_{n}}\in B_{n}\right)=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(f_{\alpha_{j}}\in B_{j}\right)
, for arbitrarily different indices
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{n}\in\mathscr{I}
and arbitrary
B_{1}\in\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{X}_{1};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{\alpha_{1}})),\cdots,B_{n}\in\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{X}_{n};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{\alpha_{n}}))
,
then
f_{j}
,
j\in\mathscr{I}
are said to be mutually independent.

Definition B.8
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{i}
,
i\in\mathscr{I}
and
\mathscr{G}
be sub-
\sigma
-algebra of
{\cal F}
. If
\mathbb{P}\left.\left(\bigcap_{j=1}^{n}A_{j}\right|\mathscr{G}\right)=\prod\limits_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(A_{j}|\mathscr{G}\right)
a.s., for arbitrarily different indices
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{n}\in\mathscr{I}
and arbitrary
A_{1}\in{\cal F}_{\alpha_{1}},\cdots,A_{n}\in{\cal F}_{\alpha_{n}}
,
then it is said that
\mathcal{F}_{\alpha}
is conditionally independent w.r.t.
\mathscr{G}
,
\alpha\in\mathscr{I}
. If
\sigma(f_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{i}))
is conditionally independent w.r.t.
\mathscr{G}
,
i\in\mathscr{I}
, then we say that the random elements
f_{i}
,
i\in\mathscr{I}
with values in
(\mathscr{X}_{i},\tau(\mathscr{X}_{i}))
are conditionally independent w.r.t.
\mathscr{G}
.

By Definitions
B.7
and
B.8
, we have the following propositions.

Proposition B.5
.

If the family of random elements
\{f_{k},k\geq 1\}
with values in
(\mathscr{X}_{1},\tau(\mathscr{X}_{1}))
and the family of random elements
\{g_{k},k\geq 1\}
with values in
(\mathscr{X}_{2},\tau(\mathscr{X}_{2}))
are independent, then
\bigvee_{i=k}^{\infty}\sigma(f_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{1}))
is conditionally independent of
\bigvee_{i=k}^{\infty}\sigma(g_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{2}))
with respect to
\bigvee_{i=1}^{k-1}(\sigma(f_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{1}))\bigvee\sigma(g_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{2})))
,
\forall\ k\geq 2
.

Proof.

Given an integer
k\geq 2
, denote
\mathscr{F}^{k}=\bigvee_{i=k}^{\infty}\sigma(f_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{1}))
,
\mathscr{G}^{k}=\bigvee_{i=k}^{\infty}\sigma(g_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{2}))
,
\mathscr{F}_{k}=\bigvee_{i=1}^{k-1}\sigma(f_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{1}))
,
\mathscr{G}_{k}=\bigvee_{i=1}^{k-1}\sigma(g_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{2}))
and
\mathscr{F}(k)=\bigvee_{i=1}^{k-1}(\sigma(f_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{1}))\bigvee\sigma(g_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{2}))).

Let
E\in\mathscr{F}^{k}
,
F\in\mathscr{G}^{k}
,
A\in\mathscr{F}_{k}
and
B\in\mathscr{G}_{k}
. On the one hand, noting that
\{f_{k},k\geq 1\}
and
\{g_{k},k\geq 1\}
are mutually independent, we know that
\bigvee_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma(f_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{1}))
and
\bigvee_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma(g_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{2}))
are also mutually independent. Since
A\cap E\in\bigvee_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma(f_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{1}))
and
B\cap F\in\bigvee_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma(g_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{2}))
, we get
\mathbb{P}(E\cap F\cap A\cap B)=\mathbb{P}(E\cap A)\mathbb{P}(F\cap B)
, which together with
A\cap B\in\mathscr{F}(k)
gives

\displaystyle\int_{A\cap B}\mathbb{P}(E\cap F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\mathbb{P}(E\cap F\cap A\cap B)=\mathbb{P}(E\cap A)\mathbb{P}(F\cap B).

(B.14)

On the other hand, noting that
\boldsymbol{1}_{F}\in\mathscr{G}^{k}
,
\mathscr{F}(k)=\mathscr{F}_{k}\bigvee\mathscr{G}_{k}
and
\mathscr{G}^{k}\bigvee\mathscr{G}_{k}=\bigvee_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma(g_{i};\tau(\mathscr{X}_{2}))
is independent of
\mathscr{F}_{k}
, by Corollary
7.3.3
in
[
64
]
, we obtain
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{F}|\mathscr{F}(k)]=\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{F}|\mathscr{G}_{k}]
, which further implies

\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{E}|\mathscr{F}(k)]\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{F}|\mathscr{F}(k)]=\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{E}\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{F}|\mathscr{F}(k)]|\mathscr{F}(k)]=\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{E}\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{F}|\mathscr{G}_{k}]|\mathscr{F}(k)],

from which we get

\displaystyle\int_{A\cap B}\mathbb{P}(E|\mathscr{F}(k))\mathbb{P}(F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=

\displaystyle\int_{A\cap B}\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{E}\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{F}|\mathscr{G}_{k}]|\mathscr{F}(k)]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{A\cap B}\boldsymbol{1}_{E}\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{F}|\mathscr{G}_{k}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}.

(B.15)

Noting that
\boldsymbol{1}_{B}\in\mathscr{G}_{k}
,
\boldsymbol{1}_{A\cap E}
and
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{B\cap F}|\mathscr{G}_{k}]
are mutually independent, we have

\displaystyle\int_{A\cap B}\boldsymbol{1}_{E}\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{F}|\mathscr{G}_{k}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A\cap E}\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{B\cap F}|\mathscr{G}_{k}]]

(B.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A\cap E}]\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{B\cap F}]

(B.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(A\cap E)\mathbb{P}(B\cap F).

(B.18)

It follows from (
B-C
)-(
B.16
) that

\displaystyle\int_{A\cap B}\mathbb{P}(E|\mathscr{F}(k))\mathbb{P}(F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\mathbb{P}(A\cap E)\mathbb{P}(B\cap F).

(B.19)

Combining (
B.14
) and (
B.19
) gives

\displaystyle\int_{A\cap B}\mathbb{P}(E\cap F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{A\cap B}\mathbb{P}(E|\mathscr{F}(k))\mathbb{P}(F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}.

(B.20)

Denote

\Pi=\left.\left\{\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}(A_{i}\cap B_{i})\right|A_{i}\in\mathscr{F}_{k},B_{i}\in\mathscr{G}_{k},A_{i}\cap B_{i}\cap A_{j}\cap B_{j}=\emptyset,1\leq i\neq j\leq n\right\}.

If
\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}(A_{i}\cap B_{i})\in\Pi
and
\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}(C_{i}\cap D_{i})\in\Pi
, then we have

\displaystyle~~~\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}(A_{i}\cap B_{i})\right)\bigcap\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}(C_{i}\cap D_{i})\right)

(B.21)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}\left(\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}(A_{i}\cap B_{i})\right)\cap C_{j}\cap D_{j}\right)=\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\left(A_{i}\cap B_{i}\cap C_{j}\cap D_{j}\right).

(B.22)

Noting that
A_{i}\cap B_{i}\cap A_{j}\cap B_{j}=\emptyset,1\leq i\neq j\leq n
, and
C_{s}\cap D_{s}\cap C_{t}\cap D_{t}=\emptyset,1\leq s\neq t\leq m
, we know that
A_{i}\cap B_{i}\cap A_{j}\cap B_{j}\cap C_{s}\cap D_{s}\cap C_{t}\cap D_{t}=\emptyset
,
(i,j)\neq(s,t)
, which together with (
B.21
) gives

\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}(A_{i}\cap B_{i})\right)\bigcap\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}(C_{i}\cap D_{i})\right)\in\Pi,

thus, we conclude that
\Pi
is a
\pi
-class, i.e.,
\Pi
is closed under the intersection operation of the set. Denote

\Lambda=\left\{M\in\mathscr{F}(k)\bigg|\int_{M}\mathbb{P}(E\cap F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{M}\mathbb{P}(E|\mathscr{F}(k))\mathbb{P}(F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}\right\}.

Noting that
\Pi
is composed of finite disjoint union of elements in
\mathscr{F}_{k}\cap\mathscr{G}_{k}
, it follows from (
B.20
) that
\Omega\in\Lambda
. If
\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}(A_{i}\cap B_{i})\in\Pi
, then we have

\int_{\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}(A_{i}\cap B_{i})}\mathbb{P}(E\cap F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}(A_{i}\cap B_{i})}\mathbb{P}(E|\mathscr{F}(k))\mathbb{P}(F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P},

which shows that
\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}(A_{i}\cap B_{i})\in\Lambda
and further gives
\Pi\subseteq\Lambda
. If
M_{i}\in\Lambda
and
M_{i}\subseteq M_{i+1}
, then we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\boldsymbol{1}_{M_{i}}\mathbb{P}(E\cap F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{\Omega}\boldsymbol{1}_{M_{i}}\mathbb{P}(E|\mathscr{F}(k))\mathbb{P}(F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P},~\forall\ i\geq 1.

Noting that
M_{i}\subseteq M_{i+1}
implies
\lim_{i\to\infty}\boldsymbol{1}_{M_{i}}=\boldsymbol{1}_{\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}M_{i}}
, by Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\boldsymbol{1}_{\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}M_{i}}\mathbb{P}(E\cap F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{\Omega}\boldsymbol{1}_{\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}M_{i}}\mathbb{P}(E|\mathscr{F}(k))\mathbb{P}(F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}.

Thus, we have
\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}M_{i}\in\Lambda
. If
M_{1},M_{2}\in\Lambda
and
M_{1}\subseteq M_{2}
, then
M_{2}=M_{1}\cup(M_{2}\setminus M_{1})
implies

\displaystyle\left(\int_{M_{1}}+\int_{M_{2}\setminus M_{1}}\right)\mathbb{P}(E\cap F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\left(\int_{M_{1}}+\int_{M_{2}\setminus M_{1}}\right)\mathbb{P}(E|\mathscr{F}(k))\mathbb{P}(F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}.

It follows from
M_{1}\in\Lambda
that

\displaystyle\int_{M_{2}\setminus M_{1}}\mathbb{P}(E\cap F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{M_{2}\setminus M_{1}}\mathbb{P}(E|\mathscr{F}(k))\mathbb{P}(F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P},

which shows that
M_{2}\setminus M_{1}\in\Lambda
. From the above analysis, we know that
\Lambda
is a
\lambda
-class, by Theorem 1.3.2 in
[
64
]
, we get
\sigma(\Pi)\subseteq\Lambda
. On the one hand, it follows from
\Pi\subseteq\mathscr{F}(k)=\mathscr{F}_{k}\bigvee\mathscr{G}_{k}
that
\sigma(\Pi)\subseteq\mathscr{F}(k)
. On the other hand, noting that
\mathscr{F}_{k}\subseteq\Pi
and
\mathscr{G}_{k}\subseteq\Pi
, we have
\mathscr{F}_{k}\cup\mathscr{G}_{k}\subseteq\sigma(\Pi)
, which leads to
\mathscr{F}(k)=\mathscr{F}_{k}\bigvee\mathscr{G}_{k}\subseteq\sigma(\Pi)
and further gives
\mathscr{F}(k)=\sigma(\Pi)\subseteq\Lambda
. Noting that
E\in\mathscr{F}^{k}
and
F\in\mathscr{G}^{k}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{M}\mathbb{P}(E\cap F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{M}\mathbb{P}(E|\mathscr{F}(k))\mathbb{P}(F|\mathscr{F}(k))\,\text{d}\mathbb{P},~\forall\ M\in\mathscr{F}(k),

which together with
\mathbb{P}(E|\mathscr{F}(k))\mathbb{P}(F|\mathscr{F}(k))\in\mathscr{F}(k)
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(E\cap F|\mathscr{F}(k))=\mathbb{P}(E|\mathscr{F}(k))\mathbb{P}(F|\mathscr{F}(k))~\text{a.s.},

thus, we conclude that
\mathscr{F}^{k}
is conditionally independent of
\mathscr{G}^{k}
with respect to
\mathscr{F}(k)
.
∎

Proposition B.6
.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be the sub-
\sigma
-algebra of
{\cal F}
. The random element
T:\Omega\to\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})
with values in
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y}),\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})))
satisfies
\mathbb{E}[\|T\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})}^{2}]<\infty
and
f\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
is a random element with values in the Banach space
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
. If
T
is conditionally independent of
f
w.r.t.
\mathscr{G}
, then

\mathbb{E}[Tf|\mathscr{G}]=\mathbb{E}[T\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]|\mathscr{G}]
a.s.

Before proving Proposition
B.6
, we introduce the following lemma.

Lemma B.4
.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a sub-
\sigma
-algebra of
{\cal F}
,
A\in{\cal F}
and
f\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
. If
f
is conditionally independent of
\boldsymbol{1}_{A}
with respect to
\mathscr{G}
, then

\mathbb{E}[f\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]=\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]~\text{a.s.}

Proof.

Let
f=\boldsymbol{1}_{B}\otimes x
, where
B\in{\cal F}
and
x\in\mathscr{X}
. Noting that

\displaystyle\sigma(\boldsymbol{1}_{B}\otimes x;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))=\left\{(\boldsymbol{1}_{B}\otimes x)^{-1}(E):E\in\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{X};\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))\right\},

(B.23)

it follows that

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{1}_{B}\otimes x)^{-1}(E)=\begin{cases}\Omega,&\text{If $0\in E$, $x\in E$};\\
B,&\text{If $0\in E$, $x\notin E$};\\
B^{\complement},&\text{If $0\notin E$, $x\in E$};\\
\emptyset,&\text{If $0\notin E$, $x\notin E$}.\end{cases},~\forall\ E\in\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{X};\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X})),

(B.24)

which leads to
\sigma(\boldsymbol{1}_{B}\otimes x;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))=\sigma(\boldsymbol{1}_{B};\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
. Since
\boldsymbol{1}_{B}\otimes x
is conditionally independent of
\boldsymbol{1}_{A}
with respect to
\mathscr{G}
, it follows from Definition
B.8
that
\sigma(\boldsymbol{1}_{B}\otimes x;\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
is conditionally independent of
\sigma(\boldsymbol{1}_{A};\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
with respect to
\mathscr{G}
, from which we can further conclude that
\sigma(B)
is conditionally independent of
\sigma(A)
with respect to
\mathscr{G}
. Given
F\in\mathscr{G}
, on the one hand, we have

\displaystyle\int_{F}\mathbb{E}[f\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x\int_{F}\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A\cap B}|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}

(B.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x\int_{F}\mathbb{P}(A\cap B|\mathscr{G})\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}

(B.26)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x\int_{F}\mathbb{P}(A|\mathscr{G})\mathbb{P}(B|\mathscr{G})\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}.

(B.27)

On the other hand, noting that
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{B}\otimes x|\mathscr{G}]=\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{B}|\mathscr{G}]\otimes x~\text{a.s.}
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{F}\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{F}\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{B}\otimes x|\mathscr{G}]\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=x\int_{F}\mathbb{P}(A|\mathscr{G})\mathbb{P}(B|\mathscr{G})\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}.

(B.28)

Then, by (
B.25
)-(
B.28
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\boldsymbol{1}_{B}\otimes x)\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]=\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{B}\otimes x|\mathscr{G}]\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]~\text{a.s.}

(B.29)

Let
f=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{B_{i}}\otimes x_{i}
, where
B_{i}\in{\cal F}
,
B_{i}\cap B_{j}=\emptyset
,
x_{i}\in\mathscr{X}
,
1\leq i\neq j\leq n
. Following the same way as (
B.23
)-(
B.24
), we have

\displaystyle\sigma\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{B_{i}}\otimes x_{i};\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X})\right)=\sigma\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}B_{i}\right).

Thus,
f
is conditionally independent of
\boldsymbol{1}_{A}
with respect to
\mathscr{G}
, which implies that
\sigma(\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}B_{i})
is conditionally independent of
\sigma(A)
with respect to
\mathscr{G}
, from which we know that
B_{i}
is conditionally independent of
A
with respect to
\mathscr{G}
,
1\leq i\leq n
. It follows from (
B.29
) that

\displaystyle\int_{F}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\boldsymbol{1}_{B_{i}}\otimes x_{i})\boldsymbol{1}_{A}\right|\mathscr{G}\right]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{F}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\boldsymbol{1}_{B_{i}}\otimes x\right|\mathscr{G}\right]\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P},

(B.30)

which leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\boldsymbol{1}_{B_{i}}\otimes x_{i})\boldsymbol{1}_{A}\right|\mathscr{G}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{B_{i}}\otimes x\right|\mathscr{G}\right]\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]~\text{a.s.}

For the random element
f
with values in Banach space
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
, there exists a sequence of simple functions with values in
\mathscr{X}
such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}f_{n}=f~\text{a.s.}
and
\|f_{n}\|\leq\|f\|~\text{a.s.}
Noting that
\|f_{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A}\|\leq\|f\|~\text{a.s.}
together with
f\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
implies that
\mathbb{E}[\|f\|]<\infty
, by the dominated convergence theorem, we get

\displaystyle\int_{F}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\lim_{n\to\infty}f_{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A}\right|\mathscr{G}\right]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{F}\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[f_{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P},

(B.31)

and

\displaystyle\int_{F}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\lim_{n\to\infty}f_{n}\right|\mathscr{G}\right]\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{F}\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[f_{n}|\mathscr{G}]\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}.

It follows from (
B.30
) that

\displaystyle\int_{F}\mathbb{E}[f_{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A}\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{F}\mathbb{E}[f_{n}|\mathscr{G}]\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}.

(B.32)

Thus, by (
B.31
)-(
B.32
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{F}\mathbb{E}[f\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P}=\int_{F}\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A}|\mathscr{G}]\,\text{d}\mathbb{P},~\forall F\in\mathscr{G}.

∎

Proof of Proposition
B.6
.

We first consider the case with
f=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes x_{i}
, where
A_{i}\in{\cal F}
,
A_{i}\cap A_{j}=\emptyset
,
x_{i}\in\mathscr{X}
,
1\leq i\neq j\leq n
. Since
T
is conditionally independent of
f
with respect to
\mathscr{G}
, it follows that
\sigma(Tx_{i};\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y}))
is conditionally independent of
\sigma(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes x_{i};\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
with respect to
\mathscr{G}
,
1\leq i\leq n
, from which we know that
\sigma(Tx_{i};\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{Y}))
is conditionally independent of
\sigma(A_{i})
with respect to
\mathscr{G}
, thus,
Tx_{i}
is conditionally independent of
\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}
with respect to
\mathscr{G}
, then by Lemma
B.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T(\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes x_{i})|\mathscr{G}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(Tx_{i})\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}|\mathscr{G}]

(B.33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Tx_{i}|\mathscr{G}]\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}|\mathscr{G}]

(B.34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(Tx_{i})\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}|\mathscr{G}]|\mathscr{G}]~\text{a.s.}

(B.35)

Noting that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(Tx_{i})\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}|\mathscr{G}]|\mathscr{G}]=\mathbb{E}[T(\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}|\mathscr{G}]\otimes x_{i})|\mathscr{G}]=\mathbb{E}[T\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes x_{i}|\mathscr{G}]|\mathscr{G}]~\text{a.s.}

(B.36)

By (
B.33
)-(
B.36
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Tf|\mathscr{G}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[T(\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes x_{i})|\mathscr{G}]

(B.37)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[T\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{i}}\otimes x_{i}|\mathscr{G}]|\mathscr{G}]

(B.38)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]|\mathscr{G}]~\text{a.s.}

(B.39)

Given a random element
f\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
with values in Banach space
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
, by Lemma
B.1
, we know that there exists a sequence
\{f_{n},n\geq 1\}
of simple functions, such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}f_{n}=f~\text{a.s.}
and
\|f_{n}\|\leq\|f\|~\text{a.s.}
If
\mathbb{E}[\|T\|^{2}]<\infty
and
f\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathscr{X})
, noting that
T(\omega)\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})
, we get
\|(Tf_{n})(\omega)\|=\|T(\omega)f_{n}(\omega)\|\leq\|T(\omega)\|\|f_{n}(\omega)\|\leq\|T(\omega)\|^{2}+\|f(\omega)\|^{2}\in L^{1}(\Omega)
, which together with conditional dominated convergence theorem gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Tf|\mathscr{G}]=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[T\left(\lim_{n\to\infty}f_{n}\right)\right|\mathscr{G}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\lim_{n\to\infty}Tf_{n}\right|\mathscr{G}\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[Tf_{n}|\mathscr{G}]~\text{a.s.}

(B.40)

By (
B.37
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Tf_{n}|\mathscr{G}]=\mathbb{E}[T\mathbb{E}[f_{n}|\mathscr{G}]|\mathscr{G}]~\text{a.s.}

It follows from
\|T\mathbb{E}[f_{n}|\mathscr{G}]\|\leq\|T\|\|\mathbb{E}[f_{n}|\mathscr{G}]\|\leq\|T\|^{2}+\mathbb{E}[\|f_{n}\|^{2}|\mathscr{G}]\leq\|T\|^{2}+\mathbb{E}[\|f\|^{2}|\mathscr{G}]\in L^{1}(\Omega)
and conditional dominated convergence theorem that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[T\mathbb{E}[f_{n}|\mathscr{G}]|\mathscr{G}]=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\lim_{n\to\infty}T\mathbb{E}[f_{n}|\mathscr{G}]\right|\mathscr{G}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[T\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\lim_{n\to\infty}f_{n}\right|\mathscr{G}\right]\right|\mathscr{G}\right]~\text{a.s.}

(B.41)

Hence, by (
B.40
)-(
B.41
), we get

\mathbb{E}[Tf|\mathscr{G}]=\mathbb{E}[T\mathbb{E}[f|\mathscr{G}]|\mathscr{G}]
a.s.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs of Lemmas in Sections 3-4

For the convenience of notational writing without giving rise to ambiguity, the subscripts of the parametrization in Banach spaces and the subscripts of the inner product in Hilbert spaces will be omitted in the sequel.

Proof of Lemma
3.1
.

Given the initial value
x(0)\in\mathscr{X}_{1}
, by Proposition
B.1
.(a)-(c), we know that
x(k)
is a random element with values in the Hilbert space
(\mathscr{X}_{1},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}_{1}))
. It follows from the random difference equation (
13
) that

x(k+1)=\big(\prod_{i=0}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(i))\big)x(0)+\sum_{i=0}^{k}\big(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\big)G(i)u(i),~k\geq 0.

Then, by Cauchy inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|x(k+1)\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\prod_{i=0}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(i))x(0)\right\|^{2}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)G(i)u(i)\right\|^{2}\right].

(C.1)

It follows from Definition
B.8
that
\sigma(u(k);\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}_{2}))
is independent of
\sigma\left(F(k);\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{1}))\right)\bigvee\\
\sigma\left(G(k);\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{2},\mathscr{X}_{1}))\right),
and it can be verified that
\sigma(\|u(k)\|)
is independent of
\sigma\left(\|F(k)\|\right)\bigvee\\
\sigma\left(\|G(k)\|\right),
which leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|G(t)u(t)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(t)\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|u(t)\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|u(k)\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(t)\|^{2}\right],~t\geq 0.

(C.2)

By the condition (
15
), we get
\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}[\|G(k)\|^{2}]<\infty,
thus, (
C
) implies
\sup\limits_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}[\|G(k)u(k)\|^{2}]<\infty
. It can be verified
\|I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j)\|\in F(k-1),\forall\ j<k-1
. Therefore, for
0\leq t<k
, by the condition (
14
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}\left\|I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j)\right\|^{4}\right|{\cal F}(t)\right]

(C.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}\left\|I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j)\right\|^{4}\right|\mathcal{F}(k-1)\right]\right|{\cal F}(t)\right]

(C.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(k)\right\|^{4}\big|\mathcal{F}(k-1)\right]\prod_{j=t+1}^{k-1}\|I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j)\|^{4}\Big|{\cal F}(t)\bigg]

(C.5)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+\gamma(k))\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\prod_{j=t+1}^{k-1}\|I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j)\|^{4}\right|{\cal F}(t)\right]

(C.6)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(1+\gamma(j))~\text{a.s.}

(C.7)

It follows from
\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}[\|G(k)\|^{4}]<\infty
and (
C.3
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\prod_{j=s+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\bigg)^{*}\bigg(\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\bigg)G(t)\bigg\|^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}\|I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j)\|^{4}\bigg)\bigg(\prod_{j=s+1}^{t}\|I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j)\|^{2}\bigg)\|G(t)\|^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(1+\gamma(j))\right)\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\prod_{j=s+1}^{t}\|I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j)\|^{2}\bigg)\|G(t)\|^{2}\Bigg]

(C.8)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(1+\gamma(j))\right)\Bigg(\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\prod_{j=s+1}^{t}\|I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j)\|^{4}\Bigg]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(t)\|^{4}\right]\Bigg)

(C.9)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(1+\gamma(j))\right)\left(\prod_{j=s+1}^{t}(1+\gamma(j))+\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(k)\|^{4}\right]\right)

(C.10)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+\gamma(k))\right)\left(1+\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(k)\|^{4}\right]\right)<\infty,\ ~0\leq s<t\leq k,

(C.11)

where the last inequality is due to
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma(k)<\infty
. By
\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}[\|u(k)\|^{2}]<\infty
, Proposition
B.1
and (
C
), we have

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{j=s+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)^{*}\left(\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)G(t)u(t)\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{X}_{1}),~0\leq s<t\leq k.

(C.12)

It can be verified that
\left(\prod_{j=s+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)^{*}\left(\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)G(t)
is conditionally independent of
u(t)
w.r.t.
{\cal F}(s),\ 0\leq s<t
. Then, noting that
\mathbb{E}[u(t)|{\cal F}(s)]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[u(t)|\mathcal{F}(t-1)]|{\cal F}(s)]=0
,
0\leq s<t
, and Proposition
B.2
implies
G(s)u(s)\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(s);\mathscr{X}^{N})
, it follows from (
C
)-(
C.12
), Proposition
B.1
.(a)-(c), Proposition 2.6.31 in
[
60
]
and Propositions
B.5
-
B.6
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg\langle\prod_{j=s+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))G(s)u(s),\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))G(t)u(t)\Bigg\rangle\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg\langle G(s)u(s),\left(\prod_{j=s+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)^{*}\left(\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)G(t)u(t)\Bigg\rangle\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg\langle G(s)u(s),\left(\prod_{j=s+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)^{*}\left(\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)G(t)u(t)\Bigg\rangle\bigg|{\cal F}(s)\Bigg]\Bigg]

(C.13)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg\langle G(s)u(s),\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(\prod_{j=s+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)^{*}\left(\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)G(t)u(t)\bigg|{\cal F}(s)\Bigg]\Bigg\rangle\Bigg]

(C.14)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg\langle G(s)u(s),\mathbb{E}\bigg[\left(\prod_{j=s+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)^{*}

\displaystyle\times\left(\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)G(t)\mathbb{E}[u(t)|{\cal F}(s)]\bigg|{\cal F}(s)\bigg]\bigg\rangle\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

(C.15)

Denote
\Lambda=\{i\in\mathbb{N}:\mathbb{E}[\|G(i)u(i)\|^{2}]>0\}
. By (
C
), (
C
) and (
C
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|x(k+1)\|^{2}\right]

(C.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{i=0}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(i))\right)x(0)\right\|^{2}\right]+\sum_{i=0}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)G(i)u(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

(C.17)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{i=0}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(i))\right)x(0)\right\|^{2}\right]+\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)G(i)u(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

(C.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{i=0}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(i))\right)x(0)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(i)u(i)\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)\frac{G(i)u(i)}{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(i)u(i)\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|^{2}\right]

(C.19)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{i=0}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(i))\right)x(0)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|u(k)\|^{2}\right]\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(i)\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg\|\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\frac{G(i)u(i)}{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(i)u(i)\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg\|^{2}\Bigg].

(C.20)

Noting that the operator-valued random sequence
\{I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(k),k\geq 0\}
is
L_{2}^{2}
-stable with respect to the filter
\{{\cal F}(k),k\geq 0\}
, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{i=0}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(i))\right)x(0)\right\|^{2}\right]=0.

(C.21)

By Proposition
B.2
, we know that
G(i)u(i)\in L^{0}(\Omega,\mathcal{F}(i);\mathscr{X}_{1})
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{G(i)u(i)}{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(i)u(i)\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|^{2}\right]=1,~i\in\Lambda,

which leads to

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)\frac{G(i)u(i)}{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(i)u(i)\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,~i\in\Lambda.

(C.22)

It can be verified that
\|G(i)u(i)\|\in\mathcal{F}(i)
from

G(i)u(i)\in L^{0}(\Omega,\mathcal{F}(i);\mathscr{X}_{1})
. Then, by (
C.3
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0\atop i\in\Lambda}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\right)\frac{G(i)u(i)}{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(i)u(i)\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|^{2}\right]

(C.23)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0\atop i\in\Lambda}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\bigg)\frac{G(i)u(i)}{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(i)u(i)\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg\|^{2}\bigg|\mathcal{F}(i)\Bigg]\Bigg]

(C.24)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0\atop i\in\Lambda}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg\|\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\bigg\|^{2}\bigg|\mathcal{F}(i)\Bigg]\left\|\frac{G(i)u(i)}{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(i)u(i)\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|^{2}\Bigg]

(C.25)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0\atop i\in\Lambda}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg\|\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\bigg\|^{4}\bigg|\mathcal{F}(i)\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\frac{G(i)u(i)}{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(i)u(i)\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|^{2}\Bigg]

(C.26)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0\atop i\in\Lambda}\mathbb{E}\left[\sqrt{\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1+\gamma(j))}\left\|\frac{G(i)u(i)}{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(i)u(i)\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|^{2}\right]

(C.27)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+\gamma(k))}\sup_{i\in\Lambda}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{G(i)u(i)}{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(i)u(i)\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|^{2}\right]

(C.28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+\gamma(k))}<\infty.

(C.29)

By the condition (
15
-
16
), (
C
)-(
C.23
) and Lemma
D.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(i)\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg\|\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}_{1}}-F(j))\frac{G(i)u(i)}{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|G(i)u(i)\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg\|^{2}\Bigg]=0.

(C.30)

Thus, substituting (
C.21
) and (
C.30
) into (
C.16
) gives
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[\|x(k)\|^{2}]=0
.
∎

To prove Lemma
3.2
, we need the following lemma.

Lemma C.1
.

For the algorithm (
11
), suppose that Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
, Conditions
3.1
and
3.2
hold. If there exists a fixed-length time period
h>0
and a constant
\rho_{0}>0
such that

(i)

\left\{I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}(a(i)\mathcal{H}^{*}(i)\mathcal{H}(i)+b(i)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}),k\geq 0\right\}

is
L_{2}^{2}
-stable w.r.t.
\{{\cal F}((k+1)h-1),k\geq 0\}
;

(ii)
\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0}\left(\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)\|_{\mathscr{L}\left(\mathscr{X}^{N}\right)}^{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}\right|{\cal F}(k-1)\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}}\leq\rho_{0}~\text{a.s.,}

then
\{I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-a(k)\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)-b(k)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}},k\geq 0\}
is
L_{2}^{2}
-stable w.r.t.
\{{\cal F}(k),k\geq 0\}
,

Proof.

Given the
L_{2}
-bounded adaptive sequence
\{x(k),{\cal F}(kh-1),k\geq 0\}
with values in the Hilbert space
\mathscr{X}^{N}
and the nonnegative integer
m
, we define a new sequence
\{u(k),k\geq 0\}
by

\displaystyle u(k+1)=\Phi_{P}((k+1)h-1,kh)u(k),~k\geq m,

(C.31)

where
u(m)=x(m),u(i)=0,i=0,\cdots,m-1
. It follows from Proposition
B.1
.(a)-(c) that
\{u(k),k\geq 0\}
is a random sequence with values in the Hilbert space
(\mathscr{X}^{N},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}^{N}))
. On one hand, from (
C.31
), by iterative calculations, we get

\displaystyle u(k+1)=

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{i=m}^{k}\Phi_{P}((i+1)h-1,ih)\right)u(m)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi_{P}((k+1)h-1,mh)x(m),~k\geq m.

(C.32)

Noting that
x(m)\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(mh-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
, it is known from Lemma
D.7
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|u(k+1)\|^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P}((k+1)h-1,mh)x(m)\|^{2}\right|{\cal F}(mh-1)\right]\right]\leq d_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x(m)\|^{2}\right],

thus,
\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}[\|x(k)\|^{2}]<\infty
implies
\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}[\|u(k)\|^{2}]<\infty
. On the other hand, we can rewrite (
C.31
) as

\displaystyle u(i+1)=\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\right)u(i)

\displaystyle+\left(\Phi_{P}((i+1)h-1,ih)-\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\left(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\right)\right)\right)u(i),

which leads to

\displaystyle u(k+1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{i=m}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\right)\right)x(m)

(C.36)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=jh}^{(j+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg(\Phi_{P}((i+1)h-1,ih)-\Bigg(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)

\displaystyle+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\Bigg)\Bigg)u(i).

Denote the
s
-th order term in the binomial expansion of
\Phi_{P}((i+1)h-1,ih)
by
M_{s}(i)
,
s=2,\cdots,h
. By (
C
)-(
C.36
), we have

\displaystyle~~~~\Phi_{P}((k+1)h-1,mh)x(m)

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{i=m}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\right)\right)x(m)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=jh}^{(j+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\right)\right)\left(\sum_{s=2}^{h}M_{s}(i)\right)u(i),

from which we get

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Phi_{P}((k+1)h-1,mh)x(m)\right\|^{2}\right]

(C.37)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{i=m}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\right)\right)x(m)\right\|^{2}\right]

(C.38)

\displaystyle~~~~+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=jh}^{(j+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\right)\right)\right.\right.

(C.39)

\displaystyle~~~~\times\left.\left.\left(\sum_{s=2}^{h}M_{s}(i)\right)u(i)\right\|^{2}\right].

(C.40)

Noting that
x(m)\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(mh-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
and
\{I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}(a(i)\mathcal{H}^{*}(i)\mathcal{H}(i)+b(i)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}),k\geq 0\}
is
L_{2}^{2}
-stable with respect to the filter
\{{\cal F}((k+1)h-1),k\geq 0\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{i=m}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\right)\right)x(m)\right\|^{2}\right]=0.

Hereafter, we will analyze the second term on the right-hand side of the inequality in (
C.37
). Denote

D(s)=a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)(\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})
. On one hand, for
2\leq r\leq 2^{h},ih\leq s\leq(i+1)h-1
, by Cr-inequality and the conditional Lyapunov inequality, we have

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|D(s)\|^{r}\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]

(C.41)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)(\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\|^{2^{h}}\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right)^{\frac{r}{2^{h}}}

(C.42)

\displaystyle\leq\max\{a(s),b(s)\}^{r}\left(2^{2^{h}-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)\|^{2^{h}}\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right.

(C.43)
