\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\left.2^{2^{h}-1}\|\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\|^{2^{h}}\right)^{\frac{r}{2^{h}}}

(C.44)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{r}\left(a^{r}(s)+b^{r}(s)\right)\left(\left(\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)\|^{2^{h}}\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right)^{\frac{r}{2^{h}}}+\|\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\|^{r}\right)

(C.45)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{r}(a^{r}(s)+b^{r}(s))(\rho_{0}^{r}+\|\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\|^{r})

(C.46)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{r}(a(s)+b(s))^{r}(\rho_{0}+\|\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\|)^{r}~\text{a.s.}

(C.47)

Denote
\rho_{1}=\rho_{0}+\|\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\|
. For
ih\leq n_{1}<\cdots<n_{r}\leq(i+1)h-1
, by the conditional Hölder inequality, the conditional Lyapunov inequality and (
C.41
), we get

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\left\|\prod_{j=1}^{r}D(n_{j})\right\|^{2}\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]

(C.48)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\left\|\prod_{j=1}^{r-1}D(n_{j})\right\|^{4}\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\left\|D(n_{r})\right\|^{4}\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(C.49)

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{1}^{2}(a(n_{r})+b(n_{r}))^{2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\left\|\prod_{j=1}^{r-1}D(n_{j})\right\|^{4}\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(C.50)

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{1}^{2r}\prod_{j=1}^{r}(a(n_{j})+b(n_{j}))^{2}~\text{a.s.}

(C.51)

On the other hand, for
2\leq s\leq h
, it follows from Condition
3.1
and (
C.48
) that

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|M_{s}(i)\|^{2}\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]

(C.52)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\left\|\sum_{ih\leq n_{1}<\cdots<n_{s}\leq(i+1)h-1}\prod_{j=1}^{s}D(n_{j})\right\|^{2}\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]

(C.53)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{C}_{h}^{s}\sum_{ih\leq n_{1}<\cdots<n_{s}\leq(i+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\left\|\prod_{j=1}^{s}D(n_{j})\right\|^{2}\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]

(C.54)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{C}_{h}^{s}\sum_{ih\leq n_{1}<\cdots<n_{s}\leq(i+1)h-1}\rho_{1}^{2s}\prod_{j=1}^{s}(a(n_{j})+b(n_{j}))^{2}

(C.55)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{C}_{h}^{s}\sum_{ih\leq n_{1}<\cdots<n_{s}\leq(i+1)h-1}\rho_{1}^{2s}\prod_{j=1}^{s}(a(i)+b(i))^{2}

(C.56)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbb{C}_{h}^{s}\right)^{2}\rho_{1}^{2s}(a(i)+b(i))^{2s}

(C.57)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{C}_{h}^{s}\rho_{1}^{2s}(a(i)+b(i))^{2s}h!~\text{a.s.},

(C.58)

where the last inequality is obtained from
\mathbb{C}_{h}^{s}\leq h!
. Noting that
\{a(k),k\geq 0\}
and
\{b(k),k\geq 0\}
both monotonically vanish, it follows that there exists a constant
c_{0}>0
, such that
\sup_{k\geq 0}(a(k)+b(k))\leq c_{0}
. Then, by (
C.52
), we get

\displaystyle\sum_{s=2}^{h}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|M_{s}(i)\|^{2}\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4h!c_{0}^{-4}\left(a^{2}(i)+b^{2}(i)\right)^{2}\sum_{s=2}^{h}\mathbb{C}_{h}^{s}\rho_{1}^{2s}c_{0}^{2s}

(C.59)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho_{2}\left(a^{2}(i)+b^{2}(i)\right)^{2}~\text{a.s.},

(C.60)

where
\rho_{2}=4h!c_{0}^{-2}((\rho_{1}^{2}c_{0}^{2}+1)^{h}-1-h\rho_{1}^{2}c_{0}^{2})
. It follows from (
C.59
) that

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\sum_{s=2}^{h}M_{s}(i)\right)u(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq h\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s=2}^{h}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|M_{s}(i)\|^{2}\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\|u(i)\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq h\rho_{2}\left(a^{2}(i)+b^{2}(i)\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|u(i)\|^{2}\right],

which together with
\sup_{i\geq 0}\mathbb{E}[\|u(i)\|^{2}]<\infty
leads to

\displaystyle\sup_{i\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|R(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

(C.61)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle h\rho_{2}\sup_{i\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|u(i)\|^{2}\right]<\infty,

(C.62)

where

\displaystyle R(i)=\frac{1}{a^{2}(i)+b^{2}(i)}\left(\sum_{s=2}^{h}M_{s}(i)\right)u(i),~i\geq m.

By the Minkowski inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69046pt\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=jh}^{(j+1)h-1}D(s)\right)\right)\left(\sum_{s=2}^{h}M_{s}(i)\right)u(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

(C.63)

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76228pt=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(a^{2}(i)+b^{2}(i)\right)\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=jh}^{(j+1)h-1}D(s)\right)\right)R(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

(C.64)

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76228pt\leq\Bigg(\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(a^{2}(i)+b^{2}(i)\right)\Bigg(\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg\|\Bigg(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}\Bigg(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=jh}^{(j+1)h-1}D(s)\Bigg)\Bigg)R(i)\Bigg\|^{2}\Bigg]\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg)^{2}.

(C.65)

From Proposition
B.2
, we know that
R(i)\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}((i+1)h-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
, thus, by (
C.61
) and Lemma
D.8
, we know that there exists a constant
d_{3}>0
such that

\displaystyle~~~~\sup_{k\geq 0\atop i\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=jh}^{(j+1)h-1}D(s)\right)\right)R(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

(C.66)

\displaystyle\leq d_{3}\sup_{i\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|R(i)\|^{2}\right]<\infty,

(C.67)

which together with that the operator-valued random sequence

\left\{I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}(a(i)\mathcal{H}^{*}(i)\mathcal{H}(i)+b(i)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}),k\geq 0\right\}

is
L_{2}^{2}
-stable w.r.t.
\{{\cal F}((k+1)h-1),k\geq 0\}
gives

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=jh}^{(j+1)h-1}D(s)\right)\right)R(i)\right\|^{2}\right]=0,~\forall\ i\geq 0.

(C.68)

By Condition
3.2
, (
C.66
)-(
C.68
) and Lemma
D.2
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(a^{2}(i)+b^{2}(i)\right)\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=jh}^{(j+1)h-1}D(s)\right)\right)R(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=0.

Given the
L_{2}
-bounded adaptive sequence
\{x(k),{\cal F}(kh-1),k\geq 0\}
with values in the Hilbert space
\mathscr{X}^{N}
, from (
C.37
) and (
C.63
), we know that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Phi_{P}((k+1)h-1,mh)x(m)\right\|^{2}\right]=0,~\forall\ m\geq 0.

(C.69)

For
j\in\mathbb{N}
, denote
m_{j}=\lfloor\frac{j}{h}\rfloor,\widetilde{m}_{j}=\lceil\frac{j}{h}\rceil
. Let
\{y(k),{\cal F}(k),k\geq 0\}
be a
L_{2}
-bounded adaptive sequence with values in the Hilbert space
\mathscr{X}^{N}
. For
0\leq i<k-3h
, noting that
0\leq k-m_{k}h<h
, from Propositions
B.1
-
B.2
, it is known that
\Phi_{P}(m_{k}h-1,\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h)\Phi_{P}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1,i+1)y(i)\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(m_{k}h-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
, by Lemma
D.7
, we know that there exists a constant
d_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P}(k,i+1)y(i)\|^{2}\right]

(C.70)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P}(k,m_{k}h)\Phi_{P}(m_{k}h-1,\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h)\Phi_{P}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1,i+1)y(i)\|^{2}\right]

(C.71)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\|\Phi_{P}(k,m_{k}h)\Phi_{P}(m_{k}h-1,\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h)

(C.72)

\displaystyle~~~~\times\Phi_{P}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1,i+1)y(i)\Big\|^{2}\Big|\mathcal{F}(m_{k}h-1)\Big]\Big]

(C.73)

\displaystyle\leq d_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P}(m_{k}h-1,\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h)\Phi_{P}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1,i+1)y(i)\|^{2}\right],~0\leq i<k-3h.

(C.74)

Noting that
0\leq\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-(i+1)<h
and
y(i)\in{\cal F}(i)
, it follows from Lemma
D.7
that

\displaystyle~~~\sup_{i\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1,i+1)y(i)\|^{2}\right]

(C.75)

\displaystyle=\sup_{i\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1,i+1)y(i)\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}(i)\right]\right]

(C.76)

\displaystyle\leq d_{2}\sup_{i\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|y(i)\|^{2}\right]<\infty.

(C.77)

By Propositions
B.1
-
B.2
, we get
\Phi_{P}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1,i+1)y(i)\in L^{0}(\Omega,\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
. Substituting (
C.69
) and (
C.75
) into (
C.70
) gives

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P}(k,i+1)y(i)\|^{2}\right]=0,~\forall\ i\geq 0,

which implies that the operator-valued random sequence
\{I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-a(k)\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)-b(k)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}},k\geq 0\}
is
L_{2}^{2}
-stable w.r.t.
\{{\cal F}(k),k\geq 0\}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.2
.

It follows from Condition
3.3
that there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
, such that
|b(k)-a(k)|\leq C_{1}(a^{2}(k)+b^{2}(k))
, which gives

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k}a(i)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k}(|a(i)-b(i)|+b(i))

(C.78)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\left(a^{2}(i)+b^{2}(i)\right)+\sum_{i=0}^{k}b(i),~k\geq 0.

(C.79)

For the integer
h>0
, denote

\displaystyle c(k)=\sum_{s=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}b(s).

(C.80)

By (
C.78
) and Condition
3.2
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}c(k)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{s=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}b(s)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}b(k)=\infty.

Denote

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}=\text{diag}\left\{\frac{1}{h}\mathscr{H}_{1},\cdots,\frac{1}{h}\mathscr{H}_{N}\right\}+\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}.

(C.81)

Noting that
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}
is positive semi-definite and
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathscr{H}_{i}>0
, by Lemma
D.5
, we know that
\mathscr{H}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}^{N})
is a strictly positive self-adjoint operator. Let
\{x(k),{\cal F}(kh-1),k\geq 0\}
be a
L_{2}
-bounded adaptive sequence with values in the Hilbert space
\mathscr{X}^{N}
, then we can write
x(k)=(x_{1}(k),\cdots,x_{N}(k))
, where
x_{i}(k):\Omega\to\mathscr{X}
,
i=1,\cdots,N
are the random elements with values in the Hilbert space
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
. Denote

\displaystyle\mu(i)=c(i)\mathscr{H}x(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)x(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)]+b(s)(\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})x(i)).

(C.82)

By (
C.80
)-(
C.82
), we get

\displaystyle~~~~\mu(i)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}b(s)\text{diag}\left\{\frac{1}{h}\mathscr{H}_{1},\cdots,\frac{1}{h}\mathscr{H}_{N}\right\}x(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}a(s)\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)x(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)]

\displaystyle=\text{diag}\left\{\frac{1}{h}\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}b(s)\mathscr{H}_{1}x_{1}(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}a(s)\mathbb{E}[H_{1}^{*}(s)H_{1}(s)x_{1}(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)],\right.

\displaystyle~~~~~~\left.\cdots,\frac{1}{h}\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}b(s)\mathscr{H}_{N}x_{N}(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}a(s)\mathbb{E}[H_{N}^{*}(s)H_{N}(s)x_{N}(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)]\right\}.

It follows from Lemma
D.6
that there exists a constant
C_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\max_{ih\leq s\leq(i+1)h-1}\Bigg(\frac{1}{h}\Bigg(\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}b(s)\Bigg)-a(s)\Bigg)^{2}\leq C_{2}\left(a^{4}(i)+b^{4}(i)\right).

(C.83)

Therefore, by Conditions
3.1
-
3.3
, the condition (
23
) and (
C.83
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mu(i)\|^{2}\right]

(C.84)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg\|\frac{1}{h}\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}b(s)\mathscr{H}_{j}x_{j}(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}a(s)\mathbb{E}[H_{j}^{*}(s)H_{j}(s)x_{j}(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)]\Bigg\|^{2}\Bigg]

(C.85)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg\|\Bigg(\frac{1}{h}\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}b(s)\Bigg)\Bigg(\mathscr{H}_{j}x_{j}(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left[H_{j}^{*}(s)H_{j}(s)x_{j}(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\Bigg)

(C.86)

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\Bigg(\frac{1}{h}\Bigg(\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}b(s)\Bigg)-a(s)\Bigg)\mathbb{E}\left[H_{j}^{*}(s)H_{j}(s)x_{j}(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\Bigg\|^{2}\Bigg]

(C.87)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{N}\Bigg(2b^{2}(i)\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg\|\mathscr{H}_{j}x_{j}(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[H_{j}^{*}(s)H_{j}(s)x_{j}(i)\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\Bigg\|^{2}\Bigg]

(C.88)

\displaystyle+2h\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\Bigg(\frac{1}{h}\Bigg(\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}b(s)\Bigg)-a(s)\Bigg)^{2}\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[H_{j}^{*}(s)H_{j}(s)x_{j}(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right\|^{2}\Bigg]\Bigg)

(C.89)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(2b^{2}(i)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathscr{H}_{j}x_{j}(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[H_{j}^{*}(s)H_{j}(s)x_{j}(i)\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]\right.

(C.90)

\displaystyle+2h\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\Bigg(\frac{1}{h}\Bigg(\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}b(s)\Bigg)-a(s)\Bigg)^{2}

(C.91)

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|H_{j}^{*}(s)H_{j}(s)\right\|^{2}|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\left\|x_{j}(i)\right\|^{2}\Bigg]\Bigg)

(C.92)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(2b^{2}(i)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathscr{H}_{j}x_{j}(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[H_{j}^{*}(s)H_{j}(s)x_{j}(i)\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]\right.

(C.93)

\displaystyle+\left.2h\rho^{2}_{0}\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x(k)\|^{2}\right]\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\left(\frac{1}{h}\left(\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}b(s)\right)-a(s)\right)^{2}\right)

(C.94)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(2b^{2}(i)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathscr{H}_{j}x_{j}(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[H_{j}^{*}(s)H_{j}(s)x_{j}(i)\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]\right.

(C.95)

\displaystyle+\left.2h^{2}\rho^{2}_{0}\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x(k)\|^{2}\right]\max_{ih\leq s\leq(i+1)h-1}\left(\frac{1}{h}\left(\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}b(s)\right)-a(s)\right)^{2}\right)

(C.96)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{N}2b^{2}(i)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathscr{H}_{j}x_{j}(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[H_{j}^{*}(s)H_{j}(s)x_{j}(i)\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]

(C.97)

\displaystyle+2C_{2}Nh^{2}\rho^{2}_{0}\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x(k)\|^{2}\right]\left(a^{4}(i)+b^{4}(i)\right).

(C.98)

Noting that
\{x_{j}(k),{\cal F}(kh-1),k\geq 0\},j=1,\cdots,N
are the
L_{2}
-bounded adaptive sequences with values in the Hilbert space
\mathscr{X}
, by (
22
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathscr{H}_{j}x_{j}(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[H_{j}^{*}(s)H_{j}(s)x_{j}(i)\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]

(C.99)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty,~j=1,\cdots,N.

(C.100)

By Condition
3.2
, (
C.84
)-(
C.99
) and Cauchy inequality, we get

\displaystyle~~~~\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mu(i)\|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}b(i)\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathscr{H}_{j}x_{j}(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[H_{j}^{*}(s)H_{j}(s)x_{j}(i)\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle~~~~+h\rho_{0}\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x(k)\|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{2C_{2}N}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\left(a^{2}(i)+b^{2}(i)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}NC_{3}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathscr{H}_{j}x_{j}(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[H_{j}^{*}(s)H_{j}(s)x_{j}(i)\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]\right)

\displaystyle~~~~+h\rho_{0}\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x(k)\|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{2C_{2}N}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\left(a^{2}(i)+b^{2}(i)\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}NC_{3}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathscr{H}_{j}x_{j}(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[H_{j}^{*}(s)H_{j}(s)x_{j}(i)\right|{\cal F}(ih-1)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]\right)

\displaystyle~~~~+h\rho_{0}\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x(k)\|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{2C_{2}N}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\left(a^{2}(i)+b^{2}(i)\right)<\infty,

where
C_{3}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}b^{2}(i)
. For any given integer
m>0
, denote
\Gamma_{m}=\{i\geq m:\mathbb{E}[\|\mu(i)\|^{2}]>0,i\in\mathbb{N}\}
. Noting that
\mathbb{E}[\|\mu(i)\|^{2}]=0
implies that
\mu(i)=0~\text{a.s.}
, we obtain

\displaystyle~~~~\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)\mu(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(C.101)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=m}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)\mu(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(C.102)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\Gamma_{m}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)\mu(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(C.103)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\Gamma_{m}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mu(i)\|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)\eta(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(C.104)

where
\eta(i)=\mu(i)\mathbb{E}[\|\mu(i)\|^{2}]^{-\frac{1}{2}},i\in\Gamma_{m}
. Noting that
\mathbb{E}[\|\eta(i)\|^{2}]=1
, it follows from Lemma
D.3
that there exist constants
M,d>0
, such that

\displaystyle~~~~\sup_{k\geq 0\atop i\in\Gamma_{m}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)\eta(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(C.105)

\displaystyle\leq M^{d}\sup_{i\in\Gamma_{m}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\eta(i)\|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}=M^{d}.

(C.106)

By Lemma
D.3
and Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)\eta(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=0,~\forall\ i\geq 0.

(C.107)

Therefore, combining (
C.101
)-(
C.107
) and Lemma
D.2
leads to

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)\mu(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=0,~\forall\ m\geq 0.

It follows from Lemma
D.9
that the operator-valued random sequence

\left\{I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}(a(i)\mathcal{H}^{*}(i)\mathcal{H}(i)+b(i)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}),k\geq 0\right\}

is
L_{2}^{2}
-stable w.r.t.
\{{\cal F}((k+1)h-1),k\geq 0\}
. Hence, from Lemma
C.1
, it is known that the operator-valued random sequence
\{I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-a(k)\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)-b(k)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}},k\geq 0\}
is
L_{2}^{2}
-stable w.r.t.
\{{\cal F}(k),k\geq 0\}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.1
.
For any given
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathscr{H}_{K}
and
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, it follows from the reproducing property of
\mathscr{H}_{K}
that

\displaystyle H_{i}(k)(c_{1}f_{1}+c_{2}f_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle c_{1}f_{1}+c_{2}f_{2},K_{x_{i}(k)}\rangle_{K}

(C.108)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{1}H_{i}(k)(f_{1})+c_{2}H_{i}(k)(f_{2}),~k\geq 0,~i\in\mathcal{V},

(C.109)

thus,
H_{i}(k)
is a linear operator.
For any given sample path
\omega\in\Omega
, we get

\displaystyle|H_{i}(k)(\omega)(f)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle f,K_{x_{i}(k)(\omega)}\right\rangle_{K}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f\|_{K}\|K_{x_{i}(k)(\omega)}\|_{K}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|f\|_{K}\sqrt{\langle K_{x_{i}(k)(\omega)},K_{x_{i}(k)(\omega)}\rangle_{K}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|f\|_{K}\sqrt{K(x_{i}(k)(\omega),x_{i}(k)(\omega))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{x\in\mathscr{X}}\sqrt{K(x,x)}\|f\|_{K},\ \forall\ f\in\mathscr{H}_{K},~k\geq 0,~i\in\mathcal{V},

then
\|H_{i}(k)\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R})}\leq\sup\limits_{x\in\mathscr{X}}\sqrt{K(x,x)}\ \text{a.s.},
which together with Assumption
4.1
gives
H_{i}(k)\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R})
.
Noting that
x_{i}(k)
is a random vector with values in the Hilbert space
(\mathscr{X},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}))
, then there exists a simple function sequence
\{x_{i}^{n}(k),n\geq 0\}
with values in
\mathscr{X}
, such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|x_{i}(k)-x_{i}^{n}(k)\|=0~\text{a.s.},~k\geq 0,~i\in\mathcal{V}.
Denote
H_{i}^{n}(k)(f)=f(x_{i}^{n}(k)),\ f\in\mathscr{H}_{K},~k\geq 0,~i\in\mathcal{V}.
Then, we know that
\{H_{i}^{n}(k),n\geq 0\}
is a sequence of simple functions with values in
\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R}).
This together with the reproducing property of
\mathscr{H}_{K}
and the symmetry of Mercer kernel
K
gives

\displaystyle\|H_{i}^{n}(k)-H_{i}(k)\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\|f\|_{K}=1}|H_{i}^{n}(k)(f)-H_{i}(k)(f)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\|f\|_{K}=1}\left|\left\langle f,K_{x_{i}^{n}(k)}-K_{x_{i}(k)}\right\rangle_{K}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|K_{x_{i}^{n}(k)}-K_{x_{i}(k)}\|_{K}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\langle K_{x_{i}^{n}(k)}-K_{x_{i}(k)},K_{x_{i}^{n}(k)}-K_{x_{i}(k)}\rangle_{K}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{K(x_{i}(k),x_{i}(k))-2K(x_{i}(k),x_{i}^{n}(k))+K(x_{i}^{n}(k),x_{i}^{n}(k))},

(C.110)

which together with the continuity of Mercer kernel
K
gives that
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\|H_{i}^{n}(k)-H_{i}(k)\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R})}=0,~k\geq 0,~i\in\mathcal{V}
. Combining this, Lemma
B.1
and Definition
B.2
gives that
H_{i}(k)
is a random element with values in the space
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R}),\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R})))
. Noting that
\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R}))\subseteq\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R}))
,
it follows that a random element with values in
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R}),\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R})))
is a random element with values in

(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R}),\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R})))
. Therefore,
H_{i}(k)
is a random element with values in
(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R}),\tau_{\text{S}}(\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}_{K},\mathbb{R}))),~k\geq 0,~i\in\mathcal{V}
.

\hfill\qed

Appendix D
Key Lemmas

Lemma D.1

(
[
65
]
)
.

Let
\{x(k),\mathcal{F}(k)\}
,
\{\alpha(k),\mathcal{F}(k)\}
,
\{\beta(k),\mathcal{F}(k)\}
and
\{\gamma(k),\mathcal{F}(k)\}
be nonnegative adaptive sequences satisfying

\mathbb{E}[x(k+1)|\mathcal{F}(k)]\leq(1+\alpha(k))x(k)-\beta(k)+\gamma(k),~k\geq 0~\text{a.s.},

and
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\alpha(k)+\gamma(k))<\infty~\text{a.s.}
Then
x(k)
converges to a finite random variable a.s., and
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\beta(k)<\infty
a.s.

Lemma D.2
.

Let
\{a(i),i\in\Lambda\}
be a nonnegative real sequence, where
\Lambda\subseteq\mathbb{N}
and
\sum_{i\in\Lambda}a(i)<\infty
,
\{b(k,i),k\in\mathbb{N},i\in\Lambda\}
be a double index real sequence. If
\lim_{k\to\infty}b(k,i)=0
,
\forall\ i\in\Lambda
, and there exists a constant
c>0
such that
|b(k,i)|\leq c
,
\forall\ k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\forall\ i\in\Lambda
, then

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}a(i)b(k,i)=0.

(D.1)

Proof.

For any given
\varepsilon>0
, it follows from
\sum_{i\in\Lambda}a(i)<\infty
that there exists an integer
N>0
, such that
\sum_{i\in\Lambda_{N}}a(i)<\varepsilon
, where
\Lambda_{N}=\{i\in\Lambda:i\geq N+1\}
. We obtain

\Bigg|\sum_{i\in\Lambda_{N}}a(i)b(k,i)\Bigg|\leq c\varepsilon,~\forall\ k\geq 0.

On the other hand, noting that
\lim_{k\to\infty}b(k,i)=0
,
\forall i\in\Lambda
, it follows that there exists a constant
M_{i}>0
, such that
|b(k,i)|\leq\varepsilon
,
\forall k\geq M_{i}
. Denote
M=\max\{M_{i}:i\in\{1,\cdots,N\}\cap\Lambda\}
. It follows from
|b(k,i)|\leq\varepsilon,~\forall\ k\geq M
that

\displaystyle\Bigg|\sum_{i\in\Lambda}a(i)b(k,i)\Bigg|\leq\Bigg|\sum_{i\in\{1,\cdots,N\}\cap\Lambda}a(i)b(k,i)\Bigg|+\Bigg|\sum_{i\in\Lambda_{N}}a(i)b(k,i)\Bigg|\leq\varepsilon\sum_{i\in\Lambda}a(i)+c\varepsilon,~k\geq M,

which gives (
D.1
).
∎

Lemma D.3
.

Let
\mathscr{X}
be a Hilbert space,
H\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X})
be a strictly positive self-adjoint operator, and
\{\mu(k),k\geq 0\}
be a real sequence monotonically decreasing to
0
with
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mu(k)=\infty
. Then there exist positive constants
M
and
d
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\geq 0}\left\|\left(\prod_{j=t}^{s}(I_{\mathscr{X}}-\mu(j)H)\right)x\right\|\leq M^{d}\|x\|,~\forall\ x\in\mathscr{X},~\forall\ t\geq 0,

(D.2)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\left(\prod_{j=0}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}}-\mu(j)H)\right)x=0,~\forall\ x\in\mathscr{X}.

(D.3)

Proof.

Noting that
H\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X})
is the bounded self-adjoint operator, it is shown that
H
has the following spectral decomposition

H=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\lambda\,\text{d}p_{\lambda}=\int_{\sigma(H)}\lambda\,\text{d}p_{\lambda}.

From the property of the self-adjoint operator, we have

\displaystyle\prod_{j=0}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}}-\mu(j)H)^{2}=\int_{\sigma(H)}\prod_{j=0}^{k}(1-\mu(j)\lambda)^{2}\,\text{d}p_{\lambda},

(D.4)

which together with (
D.4
) gives

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\prod_{j=0}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}}-\mu(j)H)x\Bigg\|^{2}=

\displaystyle\Bigg\langle\prod_{j=0}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}}-\mu(j)H)^{2}x,x\Bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\sigma(H)}\prod_{j=0}^{k}(1-\mu(j)\lambda)^{2}\,\text{d}\langle p_{\lambda}x,x\rangle,~\forall\ x\in\mathscr{X}.

(D.5)

It follows from
H>0
that
\sigma(H)\subset[0,\|H\|]
, which leads to

\displaystyle(1-\mu(j)\lambda)^{2}\leq\max\left\{(1-\mu(j)\|H\|)^{2},1\right\},~\forall\ \lambda\in\sigma(H).

Noting that
\mu(k)\to 0,k\to\infty
, it follows that there exists an integer
d>0
such that
\mu(j)\leq\|H\|^{-1}
,
\forall\ j>2d
, which further shows that
(1-b(j)\lambda)^{2}\leq 1,\forall\ j>2d
. Denote
M=\max\{(1-\mu(j)\|H\|)^{2},1:0\leq j\leq 2d\}
, we have

\prod_{j=t}^{s}(1-\mu(j)\lambda)^{2}\leq M^{2d},~\forall\ \lambda\in\sigma(H),~\forall\ t\geq 0,

and

\displaystyle\left\|\prod_{j=t}^{s}(I_{\mathscr{X}}-\mu(j)H)x\right\|^{2}\leq M^{2d}\int_{\sigma(H)}\,\text{d}\langle p_{\lambda}x,x\rangle=M^{2d}\|x\|^{2},~\forall\ x\in\mathscr{X},~\forall\ t\geq 0.

This gives (
D.2
).
Noting the inequality
1-a\leq\text{e}^{-a}
,
\forall\ a\geq 0
, we obtain

\prod_{j=0}^{k}(1-\mu(j)\lambda)^{2}\leq M^{2d}\text{e}^{-2\lambda\sum_{j=2d+1}^{k}\mu(j)}.

It follows from
\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mu(k)=\infty
that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\prod_{j=0}^{k}(1-\mu(j)\lambda)^{2}=0,~\forall\ \lambda\in\sigma(H)\cap\mathbb{R}^{+}.

(D.6)

Since
H
is the strictly positive self-adjoint operator, it follows that
0
is not in the point spectrum of the operator
H
, which gives

\displaystyle\int_{\sigma(H)}\prod_{j=0}^{k}(1-\mu(j)\lambda)^{2}\,\text{d}\langle p_{\lambda}x,x\rangle=\int_{\sigma(H)\cap\mathbb{R}^{+}}\prod_{j=0}^{k}(1-\mu(j)\lambda)^{2}\,\text{d}\langle p_{\lambda}x,x\rangle,~\forall\ x\in\mathscr{X}.

By (
D
), (
D.2
), (
D.6
) and the dominated convergence theorem, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\left\|\prod_{j=0}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}}-\mu(j)H)x\right\|^{2}=\int_{\sigma(H)\cap\mathbb{R}^{+}}\lim_{k\to\infty}\prod_{j=0}^{k}(1-\mu(j)\lambda)^{2}\,\text{d}\langle p_{\lambda}x,x\rangle=0,~\forall\ x\in\mathscr{X},

which gives (
D.3
).
∎

Lemma D.4
.

Let
\mathscr{X}
be a Hilbert space,
H\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X})
be a strictly positive self-adjoint operator, and
\{x(k),k\geq 0\}
be a
L_{2}
-bounded random sequence with values in Hilbert space. If
\{\mu(k),k\geq 0\}
and
\{\gamma(k),k\geq 0\}
are both real sequences monotonically decreasing to
0
with
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mu(k)=\infty
and
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma(k)<\infty
, then

\lim_{k\to\infty}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\gamma(i)\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}}-\mu(j)H)\right)x(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=0.

Proof.

Denote

\displaystyle b(k,i)=\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}}-\mu(j)H)x(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},~\forall\ k,i\geq 0,

it follows from Lemma
D.3
that

\displaystyle 0\leq b(k,i)\leq M^{d}\sup_{i\geq 0}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|x(i)\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<\infty,~\forall\ k,i\geq 0,

(D.7)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}}-\mu(j)H)x(i)\right\|\leq M^{d}\|x(i)\|~\text{a.s.},~\forall\ i\geq 0.

Thus, by the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem and Lemma
D.3
, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}}-\mu(j)H)x(i)\right\|^{2}\right]=0,~\forall\ i\geq 0,

(D.8)

which further gives
\lim_{k\to\infty}b(k,i)=0
,
\forall\ i\geq 0
. Noting that
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma(k)<\infty
, by (
D.7
)-(
D.8
) and Lemma
D.2
, we obtain

\lim_{k\to\infty}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\gamma(i)\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}}-\mu(j)H)\right)x(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=0.

∎

Lemma D.5
.

Let
\mathcal{A}=[a_{ij}]\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
be the adjacency matrix of an undirected connected graph
\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}
be the Laplacian matrix,
H_{i}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X})
,
i=1,\cdots,N
are positive self-adjoint operators. If

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}H_{i}>0,

(D.9)

then
\text{diag}\{H_{1},\cdots,H_{N}\}+\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}>0
.

Proof.

Given the non-zero element
x=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{N})
with values in Hilbert space
\mathscr{X}^{N}
, where
x_{i}\in\mathscr{X}
,
i=1,\cdots,N
. Here, we will prove
\langle(\text{diag}\{H_{1},\cdots,H_{N}\}+\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})x,x\rangle_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}>0
in two steps.

•

If there exists a non-zero element
a\in\mathscr{X}
with
x_{1}=\cdots=x_{N}=a
, then
x=\textbf{1}_{N}\otimes a
. Noting that
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\textbf{1}_{N}=0
, it follows from (
D.9
) that

\displaystyle~~~\left\langle\left(\text{diag}\{H_{1},\cdots,H_{N}\}+\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\right)x,x\right\rangle_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}

(D.10)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\left(\text{diag}\{H_{1},\cdots,H_{N}\}+\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\right)\left(\textbf{1}_{N}\otimes a\right),\left(\textbf{1}_{N}\otimes a\right)\right\rangle_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}

(D.11)

\displaystyle=\langle\text{diag}\{H_{1},\cdots,H_{N}\}\left(\textbf{1}_{N}\otimes a\right),\left(\textbf{1}_{N}\otimes a\right)\rangle_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}

(D.12)

\displaystyle+\langle(\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\left(\textbf{1}_{N}\otimes a\right),\left(\textbf{1}_{N}\otimes a\right)\rangle_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}

(D.13)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}H_{i}\right)a,a\right\rangle_{\mathscr{X}}+\langle(\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\textbf{1}_{N})\otimes a,\left(\textbf{1}_{N}\otimes a\right)\rangle_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}

(D.14)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}H_{i}\right)a,a\right\rangle_{\mathscr{X}}>0.

(D.15)

•

If there exist
1\leq i_{0}\neq j_{0}\leq N
, such that
x_{i_{0}}\neq x_{j_{0}}
. It follows from
H_{i}\geq 0
,
i=1,\cdots,N
that
\text{diag}\{H_{1},\cdots,H_{N}\}\geq 0
. Noting that the graph is undirected and connected, then there exist nodes
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{m}\}
such that

a_{i_{0}i_{1}}>0,a_{i_{1}i_{2}}>0,\ldots,a_{i_{m}j_{0}}>0
.
Notice that

\displaystyle~~~\left\langle\left(\text{diag}\{H_{1},\cdots,H_{N}\}+\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\right)x,x\right\rangle_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}

(D.16)

\displaystyle\geq\langle(\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})x,x\rangle_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}

(D.17)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}a_{ij}\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|^{2}_{\mathscr{X}}

(D.18)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}a_{i_{j}i_{j+1}}\|x_{i_{j}}-x_{i_{j+1}}\|_{\mathscr{X}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}a_{i_{m}j_{0}}\|x_{i_{m}}-x_{j_{0}}\|_{\mathscr{X}}^{2}\geq 0.

(D.19)

If
\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{m-1}a_{i_{j}i_{j+1}}\|x_{i_{j}}-x_{i_{j+1}}\|_{\mathscr{X}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}a_{i_{m}j_{0}}\|x_{i_{m}}-x_{j_{0}}\|_{\mathscr{X}}^{2}=0
, by
a_{i_{0}i_{1}}>0,a_{i_{1}i_{2}}>0,\ldots,a_{i_{m}j_{0}}>0
, then we get
x_{i_{0}}=x_{i_{1}}=\ldots=x_{i_{m}}=x_{j_{0}}
, which leads to a contradiction with
x_{i_{0}}\neq x_{j_{0}}
. Thus, we have
\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{m-1}a_{i_{j}i_{j+1}}\|x_{i_{j}}-x_{i_{j+1}}\|_{\mathscr{X}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}a_{i_{m}j_{0}}\|x_{i_{m}}-x_{j_{0}}\|_{\mathscr{X}}^{2}>0
. Then, by (
D.16
), we have

\displaystyle\langle\left(\text{diag}\{H_{1},\cdots,H_{N}\}+\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\right)x,x\rangle_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}>0.

(D.20)

Combining (
D.10
) and (
D.20
) gives that
\text{diag}\{H_{1},\cdots,H_{N}\}+\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}^{N})
is strictly positive.
∎

Lemma D.6
.

Let
\{a(k),k\geq 0\}
and
\{b(k),k\geq 0\}
be monotonically decreasing sequences of positive real numbers. If

\displaystyle\max\left\{a(k)-a(k+1),b(k)-a(k)\right\}=\mathcal{O}\left(a^{2}(k)+b^{2}(k)\right),

(D.21)

then

\displaystyle\max_{ih\leq s\leq(i+1)h-1}\left(\frac{1}{h}\left(\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}b(s)\right)-a(s)\right)^{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(a^{4}(i)+b^{4}(i)\right),~\forall\ h=1,2,...

(D.22)

Proof.

Let
h
be any given positive integer. Noting that
\{a(k),k\geq 0\}
and
\{b(k),k\geq 0\}
are both monotonically decreasing sequences, it follows that

\frac{1}{h}\left(\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}b(s)\right)\in[b((i+1)h-1),b(ih)],

and

a(s)\in[a((i+1)h-1),a(ih)],~ih\leq s\leq(i+1)h-1,

from which we obtain

\displaystyle~~~\max_{ih\leq s\leq(i+1)h-1}\left(\frac{1}{h}\left(\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}b(s)\right)-a(s)\right)^{2}

(D.23)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{ih\leq s\leq(i+1)h-1}\left\{(b((i+1)h-1)-a(s))^{2},(b(ih)-a(s))^{2}\right\}

(D.24)

\displaystyle\leq\max\big\{(b((i+1)h-1)-a(ih))^{2},(b((i+1)h-1)-a((i+1)h-1))^{2},

(D.25)

\displaystyle~~~(b(ih)-a(ih))^{2},(b(ih)-a((i+1)h-1))^{2}\big\}.

(D.26)

It follows from (
D.21
) that there exists a constant
C>0
, such that

\displaystyle\max\left\{(a(k)-a(k+1))^{2},(b(k)-a(k))^{2}\right\}\leq C\left(a^{4}(k)+b^{4}(k)\right),

(D.27)

which gives

\displaystyle\max\left\{(b((i+1)h-1)-a((i+1)h-1))^{2},(b(ih)-a(ih))^{2}\right\}\leq C\left(a^{4}(i)+b^{4}(i)\right).

(D.28)

Combining (
D.27
) and the monotonicity of sequences
\{a(k),k\geq 0\}
and
\{b(k),k\geq 0\}
leads to

\displaystyle\left(b((i+1)h-1)-a(ih)\right)^{2}

(D.29)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(b((i+1)h-1)-a((i+1)h-1)+a((i+1)h-1)-a(ih)\right)^{2}

(D.30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg(b((i+1)h-1)-a((i+1)h-1)+\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-2}(a(s+1)-a(s))\Bigg)^{2}

(D.31)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle h\Bigg((b((i+1)h-1)-a((i+1)h-1))^{2}+\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-2}(a(s)-a(s+1))^{2}\Bigg)

(D.32)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle h\Bigg(C\left(a^{4}((i+1)h-1)+b^{4}((i+1)h-1)\right)+C\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-2}\left(a^{4}(s)+b^{4}(s)\right)\Bigg)

(D.33)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Ch^{2}\left(a^{4}(i)+b^{4}(i)\right).

(D.34)

Similarly, we obtain

\displaystyle~~~~\left(a((i+1)h-1)-b(ih)\right)^{2}

(D.35)

\displaystyle=\left(b(ih)-a((i+1)h-1)\right)^{2}

(D.36)

\displaystyle=\left(b(ih)-a(ih)+a(ih)-a((i+1)h-1)\right)^{2}

(D.37)

\displaystyle=\left(b(ih)-a(ih)+\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-2}(a(s)-a(s+1))\right)^{2}

(D.38)

\displaystyle\leq h\left((b(ih)-a(ih))^{2}+\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-2}(a(s)-a(s+1))^{2}\right)

(D.39)

\displaystyle\leq h\left(C\left(a^{4}(ih)+b^{4}(ih)\right)+C\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-2}\left(a^{4}(s)+b^{4}(s)\right)\right)

(D.40)

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{2}\left(a^{4}(i)+b^{4}(i)\right).

(D.41)

Combining (
D.23
) and (
D.28
)-(
D.35
) gives (
D.22
).
∎

Lemma D.7
.

For the algorithm (
11
), suppose that Conditions
3.1
and
3.2
hold, and there exists an integer
h>0
and a constant
\rho>0
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)\|^{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}\right|{\cal F}(k-1)\right]^{\frac{1}{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}}\leq\rho~\text{a.s.}

(D.42)

Then
(I) for any given integer
n\geq 0
and
x\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(nh-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
, there exists a constant
d_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{m\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P}(mh-1,nh)x\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}(nh-1)\right]\leq d_{1}\|x\|^{2}~\text{a.s.};

(D.43)

(II) for any given integer
n\geq 0
and
x\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(n-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
, then there exists a constant
d_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq m\leq n+h}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P}(m,n)x\|^{2}|\mathcal{F}(n-1)\right]\leq d_{2}\|x\|^{2}~\text{a.s.}

(D.44)

Proof.

Denote
D(k)=a(k)\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)+b(k)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}
and
P(k)=I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-D(k)
. Noting that
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\geq 0
, we have
D(k)\geq 0~\text{a.s.}
Let
x
be a random element with values in Hilbert space
(\mathscr{X}^{N},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}^{N}))
, we get

\displaystyle~~~\mathbb{\|}\Phi_{P}(m,n)x\|^{2}

(D.45)

\displaystyle=\langle\Phi_{P}(m,n)x,\Phi_{P}(m,n)x\rangle

(D.46)

\displaystyle=\left\langle x,\Phi^{*}_{P}(m,n)\Phi_{P}(m,n)x\right\rangle

(D.47)

\displaystyle=\left\langle x,(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-D^{*}(n))\cdots(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-D^{*}(m))(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-D(m))\cdots(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-D(n))x\right\rangle

(D.48)

\displaystyle=\left\langle x,x-2\sum_{k=n}^{m}D(k)x+\sum_{k=2}^{2(m-n+1)}M_{k}x\right\rangle

(D.49)

\displaystyle=\|x\|^{2}-2\sum_{k=n}^{m}\langle x,D(k)x\rangle+\left\langle x,\sum_{k=2}^{2(m-n+1)}M_{k}x\right\rangle

(D.50)

\displaystyle\leq\|x\|^{2}+\left\langle x,\sum_{k=2}^{2(m-n+1)}M_{k}x\right\rangle\leq\Bigg(1+\sum_{k=2}^{2(m-n+1)}\|M_{k}\|\Bigg)\|x\|^{2}~\text{a.s.},~0\leq n\leq m,

(D.51)

where
M_{k},k=2,\cdots,2(m-n+1)
denote the
k
-th order terms in the binomial expansion of
\Phi_{P}^{*}(m,n)\Phi_{P}(m,n)
. For
0\leq m-n\leq h
, by the conditional Lyapunov inequality and (
D.42
), we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-39.83368pt\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|D(k)\|^{i}\right|\mathcal{F}(k-1)\right]\leq\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|D(k)\|^{2^{h}}\right|\mathcal{F}(k-1)\right]^{\frac{i}{2h}}\leq\rho_{0}^{i}(a(k)+b(k))^{i}~\text{a.s.},

(D.52)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~2\leq i\leq 2^{h},

(D.53)

where
\rho_{0}:=\rho+\|\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\|
. Note that

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|D(k)\|^{i}\right|\mathcal{F}(n-1)\right]

(D.54)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left.\left.\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\|D(k)\|^{i}\right|\mathcal{F}(k-1)\right]\right|\mathcal{F}(n-1)\right],~2\leq i\leq 2^{h},~k\geq n.

(D.55)

Since the real sequences
\{a(k),k\geq 0\}
and
\{b(k),k\geq 0\}
are both monotonically decreasing to
0
, it follows that there exists a constant
c_{0}>0
such that
\sup_{k\geq 0}(a(k)+b(k))\leq c_{0}
. For
0\leq m-n\leq h
, from the definition of
M_{k}
and (
D.52
)-(
D.54
), by termwise multiplication and using the Hölder inequality repeatedly, we have

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76228pt\mathbb{E}[\|M_{k}\||\mathcal{F}(n-1)]\leq\mathbb{C}_{2(m-n+1)}^{k}\rho_{0}^{k}(a(n)+b(n))^{k}\leq 2c_{0}^{-2}\left(a^{2}(n)+b^{2}(n)\right)\mathbb{C}_{2(m-n+1)}^{k}\rho_{0}^{k}c_{0}^{k}~\text{a.s.},

(D.56)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~k=2,\cdots,2(m-n+1).

(D.57)

Denote
c_{k}=2c_{0}^{-2}(1+\rho_{0}c_{0})^{2k}
, by (
D.56
), we get

\displaystyle\sum_{k=2}^{2(m-n+1)}\mathbb{E}[\|M_{k}\||\mathcal{F}(n-1)]\leq c_{m-n+1}\left(a^{2}(n)+b^{2}(n)\right)~\text{a.s.}

(D.58)

If
x\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(n-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
, substituting (
D.58
) into (
D.45
) gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P}(m,n)x\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}(n-1)\right]\leq\left(1+c_{h+1}a^{2}(n)+c_{h+1}b^{2}(n)\right)\|x\|^{2}~\text{a.s.}

(D.59)

If
x\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(ih-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
, it follows from (
D.59
) that

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P}((i+1)h-1,ih)x\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}(ih-1)\right]

(D.60)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+c_{h}a^{2}(i)+c_{h}b^{2}(i)\right)\|x\|^{2}~\text{a.s.},~i\geq 0.

(D.61)

(I) For the given integer
n
and
x\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(nh-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
, by (
D.60
), we obtain

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P}(mh-1,nh)x\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}(nh-1)\right]

(D.62)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P}(mh-1,(m-1)h)\Phi_{P}((m-1)h-1,nh)x\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}(nh-1)\right]

(D.63)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}\big[\|\Phi_{P}(mh-1,(m-1)h)

(D.64)

\displaystyle~~~\times\Phi_{P}((m-1)h-1,nh)x\|^{2}|\mathcal{F}((m-1)h-1)\big]|\mathcal{F}(nh-1)\big]

(D.65)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+c_{h}a^{2}(m-1)+c_{h}b^{2}(m-1)\right)\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P}((m-1)h-1,nh)x\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}(nh-1)\right]

(D.66)

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{k=n}^{m-1}\left(1+c_{h}a^{2}(k)+c_{h}b^{2}(k)\right)\|x\|^{2}~\text{a.s.}

(D.67)

Denote
d_{1}=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+c_{h}a^{2}(k)+c_{h}b^{2}(k))
, from (
D.62
) and Condition
3.2
, we get (
D.43
).

(II) For the given integer
n
and
x\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(n-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
, it follows from Condition
3.1
that there exists a constant
d_{2}>0
, such that
\sup_{k\geq 0}(1+c_{h+1}a^{2}(k)+c_{h+1}b^{2}(k))\leq d_{2}
, which together with (
D.59
) gives (
D.44
).
∎

Lemma D.8
.

For the algorithm (
11
), if Conditions
3.1
and
3.2
hold, and there exists an integer
h>0
and a constant
\rho>0
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)\|^{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}\right|{\cal F}(k-1)\right]^{\frac{1}{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}}\leq\rho~\text{a.s.},

then there exits a constant
d_{3}>0
, such that

\displaystyle~~~~\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=jh}^{(j+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)(\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}))\right)\right)y\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq d_{3}\mathbb{E}\left[\|y\|^{2}\right],~\forall\ i\geq 0,

for any given
y\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}((i+1)h-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
.

Proof.

Denote
D^{\prime}(k)=\sum_{s=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)(\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}))
and
P^{\prime}(k)=I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-D^{\prime}(k)
. Noting that
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\geq 0
, we have
D^{\prime}(k)\geq 0~\text{a.s.}
Let
x\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(nh^{2}-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
be a random element with values in the Hilbert space
\mathscr{X}^{N}
. Then we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}((n+1)h-1,nh)x\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}(nh^{2}-1)\right]\leq\left(1+\sum_{k=2}^{2h}\left.\mathbb{E}\left[\|M_{k}^{\prime}\|\right|\mathcal{F}(nh^{2}-1)\right]\right)\|x\|^{2},

where
M_{k}^{\prime},k=2,\cdots,2h
denote the
k
-th order terms in the binomial expansion of
\Phi_{P^{\prime}}^{*}((n+1)h-1,nh)\Phi_{P^{\prime}}((n+1)h-1,nh)
. It follows from the conditional Lyapunov inequality and Condtion
3.1
that

\displaystyle\hskip-45.52458pt\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|D^{\prime}(k)\|^{i}\right|\mathcal{F}(k-1)\right]\leq h\rho_{0}^{i}\sum_{s=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}(a(s)+b(s))^{i}\leq h^{2}\rho_{0}^{i}(a(n)+b(n))^{i}~\text{a.s.},

(D.68)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~2\leq i\leq 2^{h},

(D.69)

where
\rho_{0}=\rho+\|\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\|
. Since
\{a(k),k\geq 0\}
and
\{b(k),k\geq 0\}
are both monotonically decreasing to
0
, there exists a constant
c_{0}>0
such that
\sup_{k\geq 0}(a(k)+b(k))\leq c_{0}
. From the definition of
M_{k}^{\prime}
, by termwise multiplication and using the Hölder inequality of the conditional expectation, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\left\|M_{k}^{\prime}\right\|\right|\mathcal{F}(nh^{2}-1)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle h^{2}\mathbb{C}_{2h}^{k}\rho_{0}^{k}(a(n)+b(n))^{k}

(D.70)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2h^{2}c_{0}^{-2}\left(a^{2}(n)+b^{2}(n)\right)\mathbb{C}_{2h}^{k}\rho_{0}^{k}c_{0}^{k}~\text{a.s.},~k=2,\cdots,2h.

(D.71)

Noting that (
D.70
) and
x\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(nh^{2}-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
, we get

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}((n+1)h-1,nh)x\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}(nh^{2}-1)\right]

(D.72)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+c^{\prime}a^{2}(n)+c^{\prime}b^{2}(n)\right)\|x\|^{2}~\text{a.s.},

(D.73)

where
c^{\prime}=2h^{2}c_{0}^{-2}(1+\rho_{0}c_{0})^{2h}
. By (
D.72
), we obtain

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(mh-1,nh)x\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}(nh^{2}-1)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(mh-1,(m-1)h)\Phi_{P^{\prime}}((m-1)h-1,nh)x\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}(nh^{2}-1)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}\big[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(mh-1,(m-1)h)

\displaystyle~~~\times\Phi_{P^{\prime}}((m-1)h-1,nh)x\|^{2}|\mathcal{F}((m-1)h^{2}-1)\big]|\mathcal{F}(nh^{2}-1)\big]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+c^{\prime}a^{2}(m-1)+c^{\prime}b^{2}(m-1)\right)\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}((m-1)h-1,nh)x\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}(nh^{2}-1)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{k=n}^{m-1}\left(1+c^{\prime}a^{2}(k)+c^{\prime}b^{2}(k)\right)\|x\|^{2}~\text{a.s.},~m>n\geq 0.

Denote
q_{1}=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+c^{\prime}a^{2}(k)+c^{\prime}b^{2}(k))
, it follows from Condition
3.2
that

\displaystyle\sup_{m\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(mh-1,nh)x\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}(nh^{2}-1)\right]\leq q_{1}\|x\|^{2}~\text{a.s.}

Following the same way as the proof of Lemma
D.7
, it shows that there exists a constant
q_{2}>0
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq m\leq n+h}\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(m,n)x\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}(nh-1)\right]\leq q_{2}\|x\|^{2}~\text{a.s.}

(D.74)

For any given positive integer
j
, denote
m_{j}=\lfloor\frac{j}{h}\rfloor,\widetilde{m}_{j}=\lceil\frac{j}{h}\rceil
. Firstly, if
0\leq i<k-3h
, then
m_{k}h>\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h
. Let
y\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}((i+1)h-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
be a random element with values in the Hilbert space
\mathscr{X}^{N}
. Noting that
0\leq k-m_{k}h<h
,
0\leq\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-(i+1)<h
,
\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(m_{k}h-1,\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h)\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1,i+1)y\in{\cal F}(m_{k}h^{2}-1)
and
\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1,i+1)y\in{\cal F}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1)
, by (
D.72
)-(
D.74
), we get

\displaystyle~~~~~~\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k,i+1)y\right\|^{2}\right]

(D.75)

\displaystyle~~=\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k,m_{k}h)\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(m_{k}h-1,\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h)\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1,i+1)y\|^{2}\right]

(D.76)

\displaystyle~~\leq q_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(m_{k}h-1,\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h)\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1,i+1)y\|^{2}\right]

(D.77)

\displaystyle~~=q_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(m_{k}h-1,\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h)\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1,i+1)y\|^{2}\right|{\cal F}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1)\right]\right]

(D.78)

\displaystyle~~\leq q_{1}q_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1,i+1)y\|^{2}\right]

(D.79)

\displaystyle~~=q_{1}q_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(\widetilde{m}_{i+1}h-1,i+1)y\|^{2}\right|{\cal F}((i+1)h-1)\right]\right]

(D.80)

\displaystyle~~\leq q_{1}q_{2}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|y\|^{2}\right],~0\leq i<k-3h.

(D.81)

Secondly, it follows from (
D.74
) that

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k,i+1)y\|^{2}\right]

(D.82)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k,i+1)y\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}((i+1)h-1)\right]\right]\leq q_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|y\|^{2}\right],~k-h\leq i<k.

(D.83)

From (
D.74
) and (
D.82
), it is known that

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k,i+1)y\|^{2}\right]

(D.84)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k,i+1)y\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}((i+1)h-1)\right]\right]

(D.85)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k,k-h+1)\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k-h,i+1)y\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}((i+1)h-1)\right]\right]

(D.86)

\displaystyle\leq q_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k-h,i+1)y\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}((i+1)h-1)\right]\right]

(D.87)

\displaystyle\leq q_{2}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|y\|^{2}\right],~k-2h\leq i<k-h.

(D.88)

Finally, it follows from (
D.74
) and (
D.84
) that

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k,i+1)y\|^{2}\right]

(D.89)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k,i+1)y\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}((i+1)h-1)\right]\right]

(D.90)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k,k-h+1)\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k-h,i+1)y\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}((i+1)h-1)\right]\right]

(D.91)

\displaystyle\leq q_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k-h,i+1)y\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}((i+1)h-1)\right]\right]

(D.92)

\displaystyle\leq q_{2}^{3}\mathbb{E}\left[\|y\|^{2}\right],~k-3h\leq i<k-2h.

(D.93)

Combining (
D.82
)-(
D.89
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k,i+1)y\|^{2}\right]\leq\max\left\{q_{2},q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{3}\right\}\mathbb{E}\left[\|y\|^{2}\right],~0<k-i\leq 3h.

(D.94)

Denote
d_{3}=\max\{q_{1}q_{2}^{2},q_{2},q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{3}\}
. By (
D.75
) and (
D.94
), we have

\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi_{P^{\prime}}(k,i+1)y\|^{2}\right]\leq d_{3}\mathbb{E}\left[\|y\|^{2}\right],~\forall\ i\geq 0.

∎

Lemma D.9
.

For the algorithm (
11
), suppose that Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
, Conditions
3.1
and
3.2
hold, there exists an integer
h>0
, a constant
\rho_{0}>0
, a strictly positive self-adjoint operator
\mathscr{H}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}^{N})
and a nonnegative real sequence
\{c(k),k\geq 0\}
, respectively, satisfying the following conditions:

(i) for any given
L_{2}
-bounded adaptive sequence
\{x(k),{\cal F}(kh-1),k\geq 0\}
with values in the Hilbert space
\mathscr{X}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)\mu(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=0,~\forall\ m\in\mathbb{N},\quad\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}c(k)=\infty,

where
\displaystyle\mu(i):=c(i)\mathscr{H}x(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\left(a(s)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)x(i)\mid\mathcal{F}(ih-1)\right]+b(s)\left(\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\right)x(i)\right)
;

(ii)
\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0}\left(\mathbb{E}\left.\left[\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(k)\mathcal{H}(k)\|^{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}\right|{\cal F}(k-1)\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2^{\max\{h,2\}}}}\leq\rho_{0}~\text{a.s.}

Then the sequence of operator-valued random elements

\left\{I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{i=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}(a(i)\mathcal{H}^{*}(i)\mathcal{H}(i)+b(i)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}),k\geq 0\right\}

is
L_{2}^{2}
-stable w.r.t.
\{{\cal F}((k+1)h-1),k\geq 0\}
.

Proof.

Let
\{x(k),{\cal F}(kh-1),k\geq 0\}
be a
L_{2}
-bounded adaptive sequence with values in the Hilbert space
\mathscr{X}^{N}
. For any given integer
m\geq 0
, let

\displaystyle~~~~w(k+1)

(D.95)

\displaystyle=\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=kh}^{(k+1)h-1}\left(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}}\right)\right)w(k),~k\geq m,

(D.96)

where
w(m)=x(m),w(i)=0,i=0,\cdots,m-1
. It follows from Proposition
B.1
.(a)-(c) that
\{w(k),k\geq 0\}
is a random sequence with values in the Hilbert space
(\mathscr{X}^{N},\tau_{\text{N}}(\mathscr{X}^{N}))
. On one hand, from the definition of
w(k)
, it follows that

\displaystyle~~~~w(k+1)

(D.97)

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{i=m}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\right)\right)x(m),~k\geq m.

(D.98)

Noting that
x(m)\in L^{0}(\Omega,{\cal F}(mh-1);\mathscr{X}^{N})
, it follows from Lemma
D.8
that there exists a constant
d_{3}>0
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|w(k+1)\|^{2}\right]\leq d_{3}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x(m)\|^{2}\right]\leq d_{3}\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x(k)\|^{2}\right]<\infty.

(D.99)

Moreover, (
D.95
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle w(i+1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(i)\mathscr{H})w(i)

\displaystyle+\left(c(i)\mathscr{H}-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\right)w(i),~i\geq m,

which gives

\displaystyle~~~~w(k+1)

(D.100)

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{i=m}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(i)\mathscr{H})\right)x(m)+\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)

(D.101)

\displaystyle~~~\times\left(c(i)\mathscr{H}-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\right)w(i),~k\geq m.

(D.102)

From (
D.99
), Proposition
B.2
and the condition (ii), it is known that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\geq 0,\ i\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)\|\right]\leq\sup_{s\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)\|^{2}\right]+\sup_{i\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|w(i)\|^{2}\right]<\infty,

that is,
\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)\in L^{1}(\Omega;\mathscr{X}^{N})
. Thus, by Lemma
B.3
, we know that there exists a unique conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)]
of
\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)
w.r.t.
{\cal F}(ih-1)
. Then we have

\displaystyle~~\Bigg(c(i)\mathscr{H}-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\Bigg)w(i)=w_{1}(i)+w_{2}(i),

(D.103)

where

\displaystyle\begin{cases}w_{1}(i)=\displaystyle c(i)\mathscr{H}w(i)-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)]+b(s)(\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})w(i)),\\
w_{2}(i)=\displaystyle\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}a(s)(\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)]-\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)).\end{cases}

(D.104)

By (
D.97
) and (
D.100
)-(
D.103
), we get

\displaystyle~~~\left(\prod_{i=m}^{k}\left(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\right)\right)x(m)

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{i=m}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(i)\mathscr{H})\right)x(m)+\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)(w_{1}(i)+w_{2}(i)),

which together with Cauchy inequality leads to

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left[\Bigg\|\Bigg(\prod_{i=m}^{k}\Bigg(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}(a(s)\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)+b(s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes I_{\mathscr{X}})\Bigg)\Bigg)x(m)\Bigg\|^{2}\right]

(D.105)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\Bigg\|\Bigg(\prod_{i=m}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(i)\mathscr{H})\Bigg)x(m)\Bigg\|^{2}\right]

(D.106)

\displaystyle~~~+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)(w_{1}(i)+w_{2}(i))\right\|^{2}\right],~k\geq m.

(D.107)

By Lemma
D.3
, it is known that there exist constants
M,d>0
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\prod_{i=m}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(i)\mathscr{H})\right)x(m)\right\|^{2}\leq M^{2d}\|x(m)\|^{2}~\text{a.s.}

Noting that
\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}[\|x(k)\|^{2}]<\infty
, it follows from Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem and Lemma
D.3
that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{i=m}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(i)\mathscr{H})\right)x(m)\right\|^{2}\right]=0,~\forall\ m\geq 0.

(D.108)

By using Cauchy inequality again, we obtain

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)(w_{1}(i)+w_{2}(i))\right\|^{2}\right]

(D.109)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)w_{1}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

(D.110)

\displaystyle~~~+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)w_{2}(i)\right\|^{2}\right].

(D.111)

We now consider the right-hand side of (
D.109
) term by term. Firstly, by Minkowski inequality, we get

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)w_{1}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

(D.112)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)w_{1}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{2}.

(D.113)

It follows from (
D.95
) and (
D.99
) that
\{w(k),{\cal F}(kh-1),k\geq 0\}
is an adaptive sequence with
\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{E}[\|w(k)\|^{2}]<\infty
, which together with (
D.104
), the condition (i) and (
D.112
) gives

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)w_{1}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]=0.

(D.114)

Secondly, noting that
w_{2}(i)\in{\cal F}((i+1)h-1)
,
w(i)\in{\cal F}(ih-1)
, by Condition
3.1
, it follows from the condition (ii) and (
D.99
) that

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left[\|w_{2}(i)\|^{2}\right]

(D.115)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}a(s)(\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)]-\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i))\right\|^{2}\right]

(D.116)

\displaystyle\leq ha^{2}(i)\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)]-\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)\|^{2}\Bigg]

(D.117)

\displaystyle\leq 2ha^{2}(i)\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\big(\mathbb{E}\big[\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)\|^{2}|{\cal F}(ih-1)\big]+\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)\|^{2}\big)\Bigg]

(D.118)

\displaystyle\leq 2ha^{2}(i)\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\left(\rho_{0}^{2}\|w(i)\|^{2}+\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)\|^{2}\right)\Bigg]

(D.119)

\displaystyle\leq 2ha^{2}(i)\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\|w(i)\|^{2}\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\left(\rho_{0}^{2}+\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)\|^{2}\right)\Bigg]

(D.120)

\displaystyle=2ha^{2}(i)\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\|w(i)\|^{2}\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\left(\rho_{0}^{2}+\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)\|^{2}\right)\Bigg|{\cal F}(ih-1)\Bigg]\Bigg]

(D.121)

\displaystyle=2ha^{2}(i)\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}\left(\rho_{0}^{2}+\|\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)\|^{2}\right)\Bigg|{\cal F}(ih-1)\Bigg]\|w(i)\|^{2}\Bigg]

(D.122)

\displaystyle\leq 4h^{2}\rho_{0}^{2}a^{2}(i)\mathbb{E}\left[\|w(i)\|^{2}\right],

(D.123)

which together with Condition
3.2
gives

\displaystyle\sup_{i\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|w_{2}(i)\|^{2}\right]<\infty.

(D.124)

Thus, it follows from Lemma
B.3
that
\mathbb{E}[w_{2}(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)]
exists and

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}[w_{2}(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)]

(D.125)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}a(s)(\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)]-\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i))\bigg|{\cal F}(ih-1)\Bigg]

(D.126)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=ih}^{(i+1)h-1}a(s)\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)]-\mathcal{H}^{*}(s)\mathcal{H}(s)w(i)|{\cal F}(ih-1)]

(D.127)

\displaystyle=0.

(D.128)

Meanwhile, from Lemma
D.3
, it is known that there exist constants
M,d>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\|x\|=1\atop x\in\mathscr{X}^{N}}\inf\left\{r\geq 0:\mathbb{P}\left(\Bigg\|\Bigg(\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\Bigg)x\Bigg\|<r\right)=1\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\|x\|=1\atop x\in\mathscr{X}^{N}}M^{d}\|x\|<\infty.

(D.129)

For
m\leq s<t\leq k
, it follows from Proposition
B.2
, Proposition 2.6.31 in
[
60
]
, Lemma 3.5.2 in
[
60
]
, Proposition
B.6
and (
D.124
)-(
D
) that

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\left(\prod_{j=s+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)w_{2}(s),\left(\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)w_{2}(t)\right\rangle\right]=0.

(D.130)

On one hand, by Lemma
D.3
, it is known that there exist positive constants
M
and
d
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)\frac{1}{a(i)}w_{2}(i)\right\|\leq M^{d}\left\|\frac{1}{a(i)}w_{2}(i)\right\|~\text{a.s.}

(D.131)

Thus, by Lemma
D.3
and (
D.130
)-(
D.131
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)w_{2}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

(D.132)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=m}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)w_{2}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

(D.133)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{m\leq s<t\leq k}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg\langle\left(\prod_{j=s+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)w_{2}(s),\left(\prod_{j=t+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)w_{2}(t)\Bigg\rangle\Bigg]

(D.134)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=m}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)w_{2}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

(D.135)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=m}^{k}a^{2}(i)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)\frac{1}{a(i)}w_{2}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

(D.136)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M^{d}\sum_{i=m}^{k}a^{2}(i)\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)\frac{1}{a(i)}w_{2}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

(D.137)

\displaystyle\times\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{a(i)}w_{2}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(D.138)

On the other hand, by (
D.99
) and (
D.115
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{i\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{a(i)}w_{2}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

(D.139)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4h^{2}\rho_{0}^{2}\sup_{i\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|w(i)\|^{2}\right]<\infty.

(D.140)

Substituting (
D.139
) into (
D.132
) gives

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)w_{2}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq M^{d}\sup_{i\geq 0}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{a(i)}w_{2}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle~~~~\times\sum_{i=m}^{k}a^{2}(i)\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)\frac{1}{a(i)}w_{2}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

which together with Condition
3.2
, (
D.139
) and Lemma
D.4
leads to

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=m}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(I_{\mathscr{X}^{N}}-c(j)\mathscr{H})\right)w_{2}(i)\right\|^{2}\right]=0,~\forall\ m\geq 0.

(D.141)

Hence, substituting (
D.108
)-(
D.109
), (
D.114
) and (
D.141
) into (
D.105
) completes the proof of Lemma
D.9
.
∎
